      information that the certifying authority requires in relation to
      the application for certification, such as design specifications
      for the installation, including its systems and equipment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} conduct or assist the certifying authority in
      conducting any inspection, test or survey that the certifying
      authority requires,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of a diving installation,
      submit to the certifying authority for approval a maintenance
      program that meets the requirements set out in section 159 and a
      weight control program that meets the requirements set out in
      section 161, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving installation, submit a
      maintenance program to the certifying authority for approval;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority must determine that, in
    relation to the production site, the drill site or the region in
    which the particular installation is to be operated,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the installation, including its systems and
      equipment, is fit for the purposes for which it is to be used and
      can be operated without posing a threat to persons or the
      environment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an installation other than a
      diving installation, the requirements set out in the following
      provisions have been met:

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of these Regulations listed in
        Part 1 of Schedule 1, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of the [Canada--Nova Scotia
        Offshore Area Occupational Health and Safety
        Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-248) listed in Part 2 of
        Schedule 1, other than paragraph 22(5)(b), subsection 28(3),
        paragraph 28(5)(a), subsection 171(3) and paragraphs 172(1)(a),
        (g), (j) to (m), (o) and (p), (2)(e) and (3)(c) and (f) of those
        Regulations,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving installation, the
      requirements set out in the following provisions have been met:

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} section 174 and the provisions of Part 9, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of the [Canada--Nova Scotia
        Offshore Area Occupational Health and Safety
        Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-248) listed in Part 2 of
        Schedule 1, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the installation, including its systems and
      equipment, will continue to meet the requirements set out in
      subparagraph (i) and the applicable requirements set out in
      subparagraph (ii) or (iii), as the case may be, for the time set
      out in the certificate of fitness if

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the installation --- other than a diving
        installation --- including its systems and equipment, is
        inspected, monitored, tested and maintained in accordance with
        the maintenance program and is maintained in accordance with the
        weight control program referred to in subparagraph (a)(iii), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the diving installation, including its systems
        and equipment, is maintained in accordance with the maintenance
        program referred to in subparagraph (a)(iv);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority must

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an installation other than a
      diving installation, determine that the maintenance program and
      the weight control program are adequate to ensure the continued
      integrity of the installation, including its systems and
      equipment, and approve them, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving installation, determine
      that the maintenance program is adequate to ensure the continued
      integrity of the installation, including its systems and
      equipment, and approve it; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority must carry out the scope
    of work in respect of which the certificate of fitness is issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution --- section 155 and
  subsection 210.07(1) of Act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraphs (1)(b)(ii) and
  (iii), the certifying authority may substitute, for any equipment,
  methods, measures, standards or other things required under any
  regulation referred to in those subparagraphs, any other equipment,
  methods, measures, standards or other things the use of which is
  authorized by the Chief Safety Officer or the Chief Conservation
  Officer, as the case may be, under section 155 of the Act or
  subsection 210.07(1) of the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The certifying authority must set out in any
  certificate of fitness that it issues the details of any limitation on
  the operation of the installation that is necessary to ensure that the
  installation, including its systems and equipment, meets the
  requirements set out in paragraph (1)(b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest --- paragraph
143.2(4)(b) of Act

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 143.2(4)(b) of the Act, the extent to which
  a certifying authority may participate in the design, construction or
  installation of an installation in respect of which a certificate of
  fitness is issued is as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority or one of its subsidiaries
    or affiliates may be the certifying authority or classification
    society for the original design, construction or installation of the
    installation or any modification to it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a subsidiary or affiliate of the certifying
    authority may participate in the design, construction or
    installation of the installation to any other extent as long as it
    does not participate in any of the certification or verification
    activities in respect of the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of non-compliance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certifying authority must monitor for any
  participation beyond that described in subsection (1) and must,
  without delay, inform the person that applied for the certificate and
  the Board of any such participation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification plan

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person that applies for a certificate of fitness must submit a
  certification plan to the Chief Safety Officer and to the certifying
  authority for the purposes of the approval of the scope of work under
  section 31.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certification plan must include the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the installation that is to be
    certified, including its systems and equipment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a list of the standards that will apply to the
    installation to be certified, including its systems and equipment,
    and a list of the standards on which the measures to reduce risks
    that are described in the safety plan and the environmental
    protection plan are based or, if there are no applicable standards,
    any studies and analyses that demonstrate that the measures to be
    implemented are adequate to reduce the risks to safety and the
    environment to a level that is as low as reasonably practicable or
    to minimize the risk of hazards, as the case may be; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} other than in the case of a diving installation, a
    list of all safety-critical elements, as well as a description of
    how the associated performance standards are to be developed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of work

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  certifying authority must submit to the Chief Safety Officer for
  approval a scope of work that takes into account the certification
  plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of scope of work

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The scope of work must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the following activities to be
    conducted by the certifying authority:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} activities to verify compliance with the
      requirements referred to in paragraph 28(1)(b),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} activities to verify the validity of the
      certificate of fitness, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any additional activities to be carried out
      before the renewal of the certificate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a schedule of the activities referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of scope of work

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer must approve the scope of
  work if the Chief Safety Officer determines that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any installation, the scope of work

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is sufficiently detailed to permit the certifying
      authority to determine whether the requirements referred to in
      paragraph 28(1)(b) are met,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} describes the type and extent of reporting in
      respect of continual monitoring of the certification process being
      undertaken by the certifying authority, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} demonstrates how the certifying authority has
      complied with section 29;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an installation other than a diving
    installation, the scope of work

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} provides the means for determining whether

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the environmental criteria for the region or
        site and the loads estimated for the installation are correct,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the list of safety-critical elements included
        in the certification plan is complete and the elements are in
        place and functioning as intended,

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any installation referred to in a
        development plan, the concept safety analysis submitted under
        section 24 meets the requirements set out in that section,

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a new installation, the
        installation has been constructed in accordance with the quality
        assurance program referred to in section 100,

      - [(E)]{.lawlabel} the operations manual meets the requirements
        set out in section 157, and

      - [(F)]{.lawlabel} the installation's construction and
        installation, including the materials used for those purposes,
        meet the design specifications,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} includes the list of performance standards and
      methods that the certifying authority will use to verify
      compliance with those standards and to verify whether the
      installation, including its systems and equipment, continues to be
      fit for the purposes for which it is to be used, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} provides the means for determining whether the
      provisions listed in Schedule 2 have been complied with and
      whether the structures, systems and equipment referred to in those
      provisions are in place and functioning as intended; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a diving installation, the scope of
    work provides the means for determining whether the processes
    referred to in subparagraph 4(1)(m)(iii) and paragraph 4(1)(v) that
    are included in the operator's management system have been
    implemented.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of validity

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  certificate of fitness is valid for five years from the day on which
  it is issued if the certifying authority determines that the
  requirements referred to in paragraph 28(1)(b) will be met for a
  period of at least five years from that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Less than five years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the certifying authority determines that the
  requirements referred to in paragraph 28(1)(b) can be met only for a
  period that is less than five years, the certificate of fitness is
  valid for the corresponding lesser period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry date

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The certifying authority must indicate on the
  certificate of fitness its expiry date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period of validity

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The certifying authority may, on request of the
  holder of a certificate of fitness, extend the period of validity of
  the certificate of fitness for a period of up to three months, subject
  to the approval of the Chief Safety Officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval by Chief Safety Officer

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer must approve the extension
  of the period of validity of the certificate of fitness if the
  extension does not compromise safety or the protection of the
  environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable site or region

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  certifying authority must indicate on a certificate of fitness the
  site or region where the installation is to be operated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of fitness is valid for the operation
  of the installation at the site or in the region that is indicated on
  the certificate of fitness.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revalidation --- scope of work

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  certifying authority must revalidate the scope of work against the
  criteria referred to in subsection 31(3) and make any modifications
  that are necessary

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before renewing a certificate of fitness; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if new circumstances such as the following arise
    that have or could have a significant impact on the scope of work:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} these Regulations or the [Canada--Nova Scotia
      Offshore Area Occupational Health and Safety
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2021-248) are amended,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} new information regarding a major accidental
      event that occurred in any place is disclosed,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} amendments are made to any of the standards on
      which the certification was based, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the installation has transitioned from one life
      cycle phase to another.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revalidation approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The revalidated scope of work must be submitted to
  the Chief Safety Officer for approval under subsection 31(3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Renewal of certificate

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** The certifying authority
must renew the certificate of fitness in relation to an installation
before or on its expiry date if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority determines that the
  requirements referred to in paragraph 28(1)(b) have been met;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority has carried out the
  activities referred to in subparagraph 31(2)(a)(iii); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority has revalidated the scope of
  work and it has been approved by the Chief Safety Officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalidity

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a certificate of fitness ceases to be valid if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the certifying authority or the Chief Safety
    Officer determines that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any of the information provided under
      subparagraph 28(1)(a)(i) on the basis of which the certificate of
      fitness was issued is incorrect,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any of the requirements referred to in paragraph
      28(1)(b) are no longer being met, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any limitation set out in the certificate of
      fitness under subsection 28(3) has not been respected; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Safety Officer determines that the
    certifying authority has failed to carry out the scope of work
    relating to the installation in respect of which the certificate of
    fitness was issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice in writing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least 30 days before a determination referred to
  in subsection (1) is made, notice of the impending determination must
  be given in writing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination to be made by the
    certifying authority, by the certifying authority to the Chief
    Safety Officer and to the holder of the certificate of fitness; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination to be made by the
    Chief Safety Officer, by the Chief Safety Officer to the certifying
    authority and to the holder of the certificate of fitness.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before making a determination referred to in
  subsection (1), the certifying authority or the Chief Safety Officer,
  as the case may be, must consider any information in relation to that
  determination that is submitted by any person notified under
  subsection (2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of certifying authority

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  person that applies for a certificate of fitness decides to change the
  certifying authority in relation to an installation before the initial
  certificate of fitness is issued, the new certifying authority must
  undertake its own independent verification activities for the purpose
  of issuing the certificate of fitness.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}After issuance of certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the holder of a certificate of fitness decides to
  change the certifying authority in relation to an installation, the
  holder must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notify the Chief Safety Officer as soon as the
    circumstances permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} develop and submit to the Chief Safety Officer a
    transition plan outlining all of the activities to be carried out
    before transitioning from the outgoing to the incoming certifying
    authority and demonstrating that there will not be any gaps or
    delays in the carrying out of verification activities or any
    negative effects on the extent and quality of those activities as a
    result of the transition from one certifying authority to another;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the incoming certifying authority has
    submitted for approval to the Chief Safety Officer, in accordance
    with section 31, a new scope of work before commencing transition
    activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transition plan implementation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The holder of a certificate of fitness must ensure
  that the transition plan referred to in paragraph (2)(b) is
  implemented.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One certificate --- one authority

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} There must be no more than one certificate of fitness
  and certifying authority in relation to an installation at any given
  time.

### [Certifying Authority]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445165 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organizational structure

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** A certifying authority
must, without delay, notify the Board, the Federal Minister and the
Provincial Minister of any changes to its organizational structure,
including amalgamations and legal name changes.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports and information

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  certifying authority must submit to the Board, the Federal Minister
  and the Provincial Minister, not later than March 31 of each year, an
  annual report that contains

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the certification activities the
    certifying authority carried out during the previous calendar year
    as a certifying authority under the Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} proof of its technical capabilities and experience
    as a certifying authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monthly reports

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certifying authority must submit a monthly report
  to the Board that describes the certification activities it carried
  out during the previous month as a certifying authority under the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information and documents to Board

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On the Board's request, the certifying authority must
  submit to the Board any information obtained or documents generated in
  the course of carrying out its certification and verification
  activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record retention

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The certifying authority must retain records,
  including technical drawings, for any activity carried out during its
  certification or verification activities in respect of an installation
  until the day that is seven years after the day on which the last
  certificate of fitness issued for that installation expires.

## [PART 6]{.HLabel1}[General Requirements for Authorized Works and Activities]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1445179 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445180 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Installation manager

**[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
section 198.2 of the Act, every installation is a prescribed
installation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety and protection of environment

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** An operator must take all
measures necessary to ensure safety and the protection of the
environment during any authorized work or activity, including measures
to ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safety of persons at an operations site or on a
  support craft has priority, at all times, over any work or activity at
  the operations site or on the support craft;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} safe work methods are adopted;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} differences in language or other barriers to
  effective communication do not jeopardize safety or the protection of
  the environment;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if there is a loss of well control, all other wells
  at the same installation are shut in until the well that is out of
  control is secured;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} any equipment that is necessary for safety and the
  protection of the environment is available and in a condition to
  perform as intended at all times;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} fires can be controlled and extinguished and any
  related hazard to safety or the environment is minimized;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the administrative and logistical support that is
  provided for any work or activity includes accommodation and
  transportation and storage and repair facilities that are fit for the
  purposes for which they are to be used;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} every operations site is equipped with a
  communication system that meets the requirements set out in subsection
  129(1);

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} any operating procedure that creates a hazard to
  safety or the environment is corrected; and

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} all affected persons are informed of any correction
  made under paragraph (i).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Physical and environmental conditions

**[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} physical and environmental conditions, including sea
  states and ice movements, are observed and forecasts of those
  conditions are obtained;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the observations and forecasts are recorded each day,
  as well as each time there are substantial differences between the
  observations and the forecasts; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the records are maintained at the operations site.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location of infrastructure or equipment

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** An operator must keep
data or information that accurately describes the location of any
infrastructure or equipment at an operations site that is on or attached
to the seabed, including any abandoned installation or part of it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility, storage and handling of
consumables

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that explosives, fuel, spill-treating agents, spill containment
products, drilling, completion and well stimulation fluids and cement,
as well as chemicals and other consumables that are necessary for safe
operations, are

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} readily accessible and stored in quantities that are
  sufficient for normal conditions and any emergency situation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} stored and handled in a manner that does not create a
  hazard to safety or the environment, including any hazard that could
  result from their deterioration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Storage and handling of chemical
substances

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that all chemical substances present at an operations site, including
process fluids, fuel, lubricants, waste material, drilling fluids and
drill cuttings, are stored and handled in a manner that does not create
a hazard to safety or the environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misuse of equipment

**[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** It is prohibited for any
person to tamper with, activate without cause or otherwise misuse
equipment that is necessary for safety or the protection of the
environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of work or activity

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any work or activity ceases without delay if
  it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} endangers or is likely to endanger the safety of
    any other work or activity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} endangers or is likely to endanger the safety or
    integrity of any operations site or well; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} causes or is likely to cause pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition for resumption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the work or activity
  does not resume until it can be done safely and without causing
  pollution.

### [Document Availability]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445218 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of authorization and approvals

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator must ensure that a copy of the authorization and all related
  approvals that are required under these Regulations or Part III of the
  Act is displayed in a conspicuous location at every operations site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional copy and plans

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An operator must keep an additional copy of the
  authorization and approvals, as well as all plans that are required
  under these Regulations or Part III of the Act, at every operations
  site and must ensure that they are readily accessible for consultation
  or examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency response procedures and other
documentation

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a copy of the most current version of the emergency response
procedures and any documentation that is necessary to carry out an
authorized work or activity and to operate and maintain an installation
or pipeline is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} readily accessible at all times at every operations
  site and emergency response operations centre; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} usable under all foreseeable circumstances at each
  location referred to in paragraph (a).

### [Plans]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445228 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the safety plan referred to in section 9,
  the environmental protection plan referred to in section 10 and the
  resource management plan referred to in section 25 are implemented at
  the commencement of any work or activity and that the contingency plan
  referred to in section 11 is implemented as soon as an accidental
  event occurs or appears imminent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periodic updates

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the safety plan,
  environmental protection plan, resource management plan and
  contingency plan are periodically updated; however, the descriptions
  of installations, vessels, systems and equipment that are included in
  the safety plan and the environmental protection plan as required by
  paragraphs 9(2)(c) and 10(2)(c), respectively, must be updated as soon
  as the circumstances permit after the modification, replacement or
  addition of any major component.

## [PART 7]{.HLabel1}[Geoscientific Programs, Geotechnical Programs and Environmental Programs]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1445234 .Part}

### [Equipment, Materials and Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445235 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} all equipment and materials that are necessary to
  conduct a geoscientific program, geotechnical program or environmental
  program are handled, installed, inspected, tested, maintained and
  operated in a manner that takes into account the manufacturer's
  instructions and industry standards and best practices; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if any of the equipment, its components or the
  materials are defective, they are, without delay, repaired or replaced
  in accordance with the manufacturer's recommendations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification

**[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a competent third party has certified that all equipment that is
installed temporarily on a vessel to conduct a geoscientific program,
geotechnical program or environmental program is fit for the purposes
for which it is to be used.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damage to property

**[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** An operator must take all
necessary measures to ensure that no property is damaged as a result of
a geoscientific program, geotechnical program or environmental program.

### [Energy Sources]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445244 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General requirements

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any energy source that is used in a
  geoscientific program, geotechnical program or environmental program
  is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} kept free from any substance that could create a
    hazard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} operated in a manner that prevents inadvertent
    activation of the energy source.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electrical or electromagnetic energy
  source

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any electrical or
  electromagnetic energy source is equipped with circuit breakers on the
  charging and discharging circuits and with wiring that is adequately
  insulated and grounded to prevent current leakage and electrical
  shock.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Elimination of risk to divers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the program is
  conducted in a manner that eliminates all safety risks to divers from
  any energy source used, including by determining the minimum distances
  that are required to be maintained between the divers and the energy
  source and ensuring compliance with those distances.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testing of energy sources

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must minimize energy source testing on the deck of an
  operations site while a geoscientific program, geotechnical program or
  environmental program is being conducted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Energy source activation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before an energy source is activated for testing
  purposes, the operator must ensure that measures are taken to protect
  persons at the operations site where the test will be conducted from
  exposure to any hazard associated with the energy source, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} advising those persons that a test will be
    conducted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} safely securing all equipment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an electrical or electromagnetic
    energy source, fully immersing it in water.

### [Primary Vessel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445262 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that the primary vessel used in a geoscientific program, geotechnical
program or environmental program holds a valid certificate of class
issued by a classification society.

### [Destruction, Discard or Removal from Canada]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445265 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibited without approval

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It is
  prohibited for any person to destroy, discard or, subject to
  subsection (2), remove from Canada the following materials and
  information that are obtained in the context of a geoscientific
  program, geotechnical program or environmental program unless the
  destruction, discard or removal is approved by the Board under
  subsection (3):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all field data and final processed data that are in
    a digital format, together with a description of that data format;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any samples; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all other data, observations, readings and
    supporting information obtained during the program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The materials and information may be removed from
  Canada without the approval of the Board for the purpose of being
  processed in a foreign country if they are returned to Canada as soon
  as the processing is complete.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the day on which the Board
  receives an application for approval to destroy, discard or remove
  from Canada materials or information, the Board must approve the
  application if the Board is satisfied that the materials or
  information are not of much use or value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of materials or information

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Board may, after receiving an application
  referred to in subsection (3), require that the materials or
  information, or a copy of the information, be provided to the Board
  within the period that it specifies.

## [PART 8]{.HLabel1}[Drilling and Production]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1445278 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445279 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allocation of areas

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** The Board may make orders
respecting the allocation of areas, including respecting the
determination of the size of spacing units and the determination of well
production rates, for the purpose of drilling for or producing
petroleum.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Name, classification or status of well

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** The Board may give a
name, classification or status to any well and may change that name,
classification or status.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pool, zone or field

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** The Board may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} designate a zone as such for the purposes of these
  Regulations;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} give a name to a pool, zone or field and change that
  name; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} define the boundaries of a pool, zone or field.

### [Evaluation of Wells, Pools and Fields]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445289 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data acquisition programs

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the field data acquisition program referred
  to in section 13 and the well data acquisition program referred to in
  section 18 are implemented in accordance with good oilfield practices.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Partial implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If part of the field or well data acquisition program
  cannot be implemented, the operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a conservation officer is notified as soon as the
    circumstances permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} measures to otherwise achieve the goals of the
    program are submitted to the Board for approval; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the measures approved by the Board are implemented.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board approval of alternate measures

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board must approve the measures submitted under
  paragraph (2)(b) if the operator demonstrates that the measures can
  achieve the goals of the field data acquisition program or the well
  data acquisition program, as the case may be, or are the only ones
  that can be taken in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periodic updates

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the field data
  acquisition program is periodically updated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation evaluation, testing and
sampling

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** If the Board determines
that data or samples from a formation in a well would contribute
substantially to the geological and reservoir evaluation, the operator
must ensure that the formation is evaluated, tested and sampled as
necessary to obtain the data or samples.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation flow test

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that no development well is put into production
  unless a formation flow test that has been approved by the Board under
  subsection (5) is conducted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Well operation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a development well is subjected to a well
  operation that might change its deliverability, productivity or
  injectivity, the operator must, for the purpose of determining the
  effects of the operation on the well's deliverability, productivity or
  injectivity, ensure that a formation flow test that has been approved
  by the Board under subsection (5) is conducted as soon as the
  circumstances permit after the well operation has ended and the flow
  or injection conditions have stabilized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before conducting a formation flow test on a well
  drilled on a geological feature, the operator must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} submit a formation flow test program to the Board;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} obtain the Board's approval under subsection (5) to
    conduct the formation flow test.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contribution to geological and
  reservoir evaluation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Board may require that the operator conduct a
  formation flow test on a well drilled on a geological feature, other
  than the first well, if the Board determines that the test would
  contribute to the geological and reservoir evaluation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of formation flow test

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Board must approve a formation flow test if the
  operator demonstrates that the test will be conducted in a manner that
  ensures safety and the protection of the environment and in accordance
  with good oilfield practices and that the test will enable the
  operator to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain data on the deliverability of the reservoir
    and the productivity of the well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish the characteristics of the reservoir; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} obtain representative samples of the formation
    fluids.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Samples and cores

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that all drill cutting and fluid samples and
  cores collected as part of the field data acquisition program referred
  to in section 13 and the well data acquisition program referred to in
  section 18 are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stored in durable containers that are correctly
    labelled for identification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} transported and stored in a manner that prevents
    any loss or deterioration; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} delivered to the Board within 60 days after the day
    on which the well is abandoned, suspended or completed, unless the
    analyses are ongoing, in which case the samples or cores, or any
    remaining parts, are to be delivered to the Board on completion of
    the analyses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remaining conventional core

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An operator must ensure that, after any samples
  necessary for analysis or for research or academic studies have been
  removed from a conventional core, the remaining core, or a
  longitudinal slab that is not less than one half of the
  cross-sectional area of that core, is delivered to the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remaining sidewall core

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, after any samples
  necessary for analysis or for research or academic studies have been
  removed from a sidewall core, the remaining core is delivered to the
  Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice before disposal

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** Before disposing of any
drill cutting or fluid samples, cores or evaluation data, an operator
must ensure that the Board is notified in writing and given an
opportunity to request delivery of the samples, cores or data.

### [Location of Wells]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445332 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Depth measurements

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that no record is made of any depth in a well unless the depth is
measured from the rotary table of the drilling rig.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directional and deviation surveys

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} directional and deviation surveys are taken at
  intervals that allow the position of the well-bore to be accurately
  known during drilling;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the directional and deviation surveys are adequate to
  permit the management, in relation to the well-bore, of identified
  geohazards, the intersection of the geological targets for the well
  and the intersection of the well-bore in the event that a relief well
  is required; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of a relief well, every well is
  drilled in compliance with internationally recognized well-bore
  collision avoidance practices and procedures and in a manner that does
  not intersect an existing well.

### [Well Integrity]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445340 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Well control

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that adequate procedures, materials and equipment
  are in place and used throughout the life cycle of the well to prevent
  the loss of well control.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliable well control equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The equipment referred to in subsection (1) must
  include reliable well control equipment to detect and control kicks,
  prevent blowouts and safely conduct all well operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shallow hazards

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} During well operations conducted without a riser, the
  operator must ensure that measures are implemented to reduce the risk
  of shallow hazards while drilling.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surface casing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the surface casing of
  the well is installed to a sufficient depth, and in a competent
  formation, to establish well control for the continuation of the
  drilling operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Blowout preventer and barrier envelopes

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} After the surface casing has been installed and
  cemented, the operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a blowout preventer is installed before the casing
    shoe is drilled out; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there are at least two independent barrier
    envelopes --- each of which is to be verified by the operator --- in
    place throughout the life cycle of the well.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barrier envelope failure

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If there is a failure in a barrier envelope, the
  operator must ensure that no well operation, other than one that is
  intended to replace or restore the barrier envelope, takes place until
  the barrier envelope is replaced or restored.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement or restoration of barrier
  envelope

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the barrier envelope is replaced or restored as
    soon as the circumstances permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every effort is made for the replacement or
    restoration to conform to the original design specifications; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the barrier envelope is verified after its
    replacement or restoration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drilling fluid column

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, during well
  operations, one of the two barrier envelopes is the drilling fluid
  column, except when drilling under-balanced or if, when a completion
  or test string is run, the other barrier envelope has already been
  installed downhole and tested.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pressure control equipment

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all pressure control
  equipment associated with well operations is pressure-tested on
  installation and as often as necessary to ensure its continued safe
  operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective measures

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} If well control is lost or if safety, the protection
  of the environment or resource conservation is at risk, the operator
  must ensure that any necessary corrective measures are taken without
  delay.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Casing and wellhead system

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a casing and wellhead system is designed,
  taking into account the wellhead's fatigue life, so that, throughout
  the life cycle of the well,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the well can be drilled safely, targeted formations
    can be evaluated and developed and waste can be prevented;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum conditions, forces and stresses to
    which the casing and wellhead system may be subjected are withstood;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the integrity of gas hydrate and permafrost zones
    is protected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barrier analysis

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, during the design of
  the casing and wellhead system, if the annulus is to be used for fluid
  production or injection, a barrier analysis is conducted to confirm
  that two barrier envelopes can be maintained in place throughout the
  life cycle of the well.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Casing depth

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that each casing is
  installed at a depth that provides for adequate kick tolerance and
  safe well control.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wellhead fatigue life

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that well operations do not
  continue beyond the wellhead's fatigue life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cement slurry

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the cement slurry is
  designed and installed so that, throughout the life cycle of the well,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the movement of formation fluids is prevented and,
    when required for safety, resource evaluation or waste prevention,
    the isolation of the petroleum and water zones is ensured;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} support for the casing is provided;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} corrosion of the casing over the cemented interval
    is minimized; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the integrity of gas hydrate and permafrost zones
    is protected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cement integrity and placement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the cement integrity
  and placement are verified, subject to subsection (7), through
  pressure-testing and, if the cement is a common barrier element of the
  two barrier envelopes or if confirmation of zonal isolation is
  required, also through logging.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other methods of verification

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The cement integrity and placement may be verified
  using other methods if the operator demonstrates that those methods
  provide a level of verification that is equivalent to those referred
  to in subsection (6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cement design and slurry analysis

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the cement design is
  subjected to comprehensive laboratory testing and pre-cementing
  quality control, under all foreseeable conditions that could have an
  impact on cementing, so that the cement provides the expected
  isolation and can be efficiently installed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiting on cement time

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, after cementing any
  casing or casing liner and before drilling out the casing shoe, the
  cement reaches the minimum compressive strength sufficient to support
  the casing and provide zonal isolation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Casing pressure testing

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, after any casing is
  installed and cemented and before the casing shoe is drilled out, the
  casing is pressure-tested to the value required to confirm its
  integrity for maximum anticipated operating pressure throughout the
  life cycle of the well.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation leak-off or integrity test

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a formation leak-off test or a formation
  integrity test is conducted

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before drilling more than 10 m of new formation
    below the shoe of any casing other than the conductor casing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before drilling more than 10 m when sidetracking
    from the previous casing string.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pressure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The formation leak-off test or formation integrity
  test must be conducted at a pressure that allows for safe drilling to
  the next casing depth and for the adequacy of the cement at the level
  of the shoe to be verified before drilling ahead.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Completion, testing and operation of
development wells

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator of a development well must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the well is completed, tested and operated in a
    safe manner that allows for maximum recovery of petroleum without
    waste or pollution throughout the life cycle of the well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of commingled production, each
    completion interval is isolated from any other porous or permeable
    interval penetrated by the well;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the production of sand, carbonate or
    other solids is controlled and does not create a safety hazard or
    cause waste;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the setting depth of each packer is as deep as
    possible and is such that any leak through the production casing
    below the packer will be contained by the barrier envelope outside
    the casing;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the formation and any annulus seal can withstand
    the pressures and temperatures expected throughout the life cycle of
    the well;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if practicable, any mechanical well condition that
    may have an adverse effect on the production of petroleum from, or
    the injection of fluids into, the well is corrected;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the injection or production profile of the well is
    improved or the completion interval of the well is changed if it is
    necessary to do so to prevent waste;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} if different pressure and inflow characteristics of
    two or more pools might adversely affect the recovery of petroleum
    from any of those pools, the well is operated as a single pool well
    or as a segregated multi-pool well;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} during completion operations and before the removal
    of pressure control equipment and handover for operations, all
    barrier elements are tested to the maximum pressure to which they
    are anticipated to be subjected and, if possible, pressure testing
    is in the direction of flow; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} following any workover or intervention, any
    affected barrier elements are pressure-tested.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Segregated multi-pool well

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the development well is a segregated multi-pool
  well, the operator must also ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after the well is completed, segregation within and
    outside the well casing is verified; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there is reason to doubt that segregation is
    being maintained, a segregation test is conducted as soon as the
    circumstances permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[multi-pool
  well]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[multi-pool
  well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a well that is completed in more than
  one pool.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production tubing

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that the production tubing used in a well is designed and maintained to
be compatible with the fluids to which it will be exposed, to withstand
the maximum conditions, forces and stresses to which it may be subjected
and to maximize recovery of petroleum from the pool.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safe operations and production

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that equipment and procedures are in place to recognize and control
normal and abnormal operating conditions, for the purposes of allowing
for safe and controlled well operations and production and of preventing
pollution.

### [Measurements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445425 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Flow and volume

- **[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  operator must ensure that the following are measured:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the rate of flow and the volume of the fluid that
    is produced from each well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rate of flow and the volume of the fluid or
    waste material that is injected into each well; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the volume of the fluid that is produced from each
    well that is used, flared, vented, burned or otherwise disposed of.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternate measurements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Alternate measurements may be conducted if approved
  by the Board under section 14.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Method

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all measurements are
  conducted using the flow system, flow calculation procedure and flow
  allocation procedure approved under subsection 14(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allocation of group production

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that group production of oil, gas and water from wells and the volume of
fluids injected into those wells are allocated on a [pro
rata]{.otherLang lang="la"} basis using the flow system, flow
calculation procedure and flow allocation procedure approved under
subsection 14(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allocation over multiple pools or zones

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  well is completed over multiple pools or zones, the operator must
  ensure that the production of oil, gas and water from the well and the
  volume of fluids injected into the well are allocated on a [pro
  rata]{.otherLang lang="la"} basis to the pools or zones using the flow
  allocation procedure approved under subsection 14(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proration tests

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that sufficient proration
  tests are conducted to measure the rates at which fluids are produced
  from the well to ensure that the allocation of oil, gas and water
  production to the pools and zones as a result of the flow allocation
  procedure is accurate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testing and maintenance

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} meters and other associated components of the flow
    system are calibrated and maintained to ensure their accuracy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the equipment used to calibrate the flow system is
    calibrated in accordance with good measurement practices; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any component of the flow system that may have an
    impact on the accuracy or integrity of the flow system and that is
    not functioning in accordance with the manufacturer's specifications
    is repaired or replaced without delay or, if it is not possible to
    do so without delay, corrective measures are taken to minimize the
    impact on the accuracy and integrity of the flow system while the
    repair or replacement is in progress.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a conservation officer
  is notified, as soon as the circumstances permit, of any modification
  to or malfunction or failure of any flow system component that may
  have an impact on the accuracy of the flow system and of the
  corrective measures taken.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calibration

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a conservation officer is notified of the calibration
  of any transfer meter prover or master meter used in conjunction with
  a transfer meter at least 30 days before the day on which it is
  calibrated or as agreed to in writing by the Chief Conservation
  Officer; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} following completion of the calibration, a copy of
  the calibration certificate is submitted to the Chief Conservation
  Officer as soon as the circumstances permit.

### [Production Conservation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445455 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resource management

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** An operator must, in
respect of the recovery of petroleum, ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} recovery from a pool or zone is maximized in
  accordance with good oilfield practices;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} wells are located and operated to provide for maximum
  recovery from a pool or zone; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there is reason to believe that infill drilling or
  the implementation of an enhanced recovery plan might result in
  increased recovery from a pool or field, studies on those methods are
  conducted and submitted to the Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commingled production

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It is
  prohibited for an operator to engage in commingled production unless
  approved by the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval by the Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board must approve commingled production if the
  operator demonstrates that it will maximize the recovery of petroleum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measurement and allocation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the operator engages in commingled production, it
  must ensure that the total volume and the rate of production of each
  fluid produced is measured and the volume from each pool or zone is
  allocated in accordance with the requirements set out in sections 74
  to 78.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pilot scheme

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator may develop and implement a pilot scheme that applies
  technology in relation to the commercial production of petroleum from
  a pool, field or zone that is accessible from a production
  installation and in relation to which there is an approved development
  plan for the purpose of obtaining information on reservoir, production
  or technology performance in order to optimize production performance
  under the development plan or to determine whether the development
  plan requires an amendment for production performance to be optimized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration and interim evaluations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board must establish

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the pilot scheme, based on the time
    required to achieve the stated objectives; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the intervals at which interim evaluations of the
    pilot scheme are to be conducted and reported to the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Completion of pilot scheme

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the pilot scheme, the operator must
  ensure that any production activities undertaken for the purpose of
  the scheme are discontinued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition against flaring or venting

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** It is prohibited for an
operator to flare or vent gas unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Board authorizes flaring or venting as part of
  the authorization;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the flaring or venting occurs during a formation flow
  test approved by the Board under subsection 63(5); or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} it is necessary in order to remediate an emergency
  situation that may cause serious risk to human health or safety and
  the Board is notified, as soon as the circumstances permit, of the
  flaring or venting and of the volume flared or vented.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Venting limit

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the volume of gas vented under paragraph
  82(a) per installation during a year is not greater than 15 000
  standard m^3^.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[vented]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1),
  [[vented]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means emitted in a controlled manner,
  other than as a result of combustion, from an installation due to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the design of equipment or operational procedures
    at the installation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the occurrence of an event that pressurizes the gas
    beyond the capacity of the equipment at the installation to retain
    the gas.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gas emissions

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator must ensure that the emissions of gas from the seals of a
  centrifugal compressor or reciprocating compressor at an installation
  are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} captured and routed to gas conservation equipment
    or gas destruction equipment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} routed to vents that release those emissions into
    the atmosphere.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Flow rate measurement device

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the flow rate of
  emissions of gas released from vents referred to in paragraph (1)(b)
  is measured by means of a continuous monitoring device that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} calibrated in accordance with the manufacturer's
    recommendations such that its measurements have a maximum margin of
    error of ±10%;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} operated continuously, other than during periods
    when it is undergoing normal servicing or timely repairs; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} equipped with an alarm that is triggered when the
    applicable flow rate limit referred to in subsections (3) and (4)
    for the vents of the compressor is reached.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Flow rate limit --- centrifugal
  compressor

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the flow rate limit of
  emissions from the vents of a centrifugal compressor on an
  installation is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a compressor that is installed
    before January 1, 2023,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 0.68 standard m^3^/min if the compressor has a
      rated brake power of greater than or equal to 5 MW, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 0.34 standard m^3^/min if the compressor has a
      rated brake power of less than 5 MW; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a compressor that is installed on or
    after January 1, 2023, 0.14 standard m^3^/min.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Flow rate limit --- reciprocating
  compressor

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the flow rate limit of
  emissions that are from the rod packings and distance pieces of a
  reciprocating compressor on an installation is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the compressor is installed before January 1,
    2023, the product of 0.023 standard m^3^/min and the number of
    pressurized cylinders that the compressor has; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the compressor is installed on or after January
    1, 2023, the product of 0.001 standard m^3^/min and the number of
    pressurized cylinders that the compressor has.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective measures

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the alarm referred to in paragraph (2)(c) is
  triggered, the operator must ensure that corrective measures are taken
  as soon as the circumstances permit to reduce the flow rate to below
  or equal to the applicable flow rate limit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition against oil burning

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** It is prohibited for an
operator to burn oil unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Board authorizes burning as part of the
  authorization;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the burning occurs during a formation flow test
  approved by the Board under subsection 63(5); or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} it is necessary in order to remediate an emergency
  situation that may cause serious risk to human health or safety and
  the Board is notified, as soon as the circumstances permit, of the
  burning and of the amount burned.

### [Spill-treating Agent]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445516 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of net environmental
benefit

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** In determining for the
purpose of subsection 166.1(3) of the Act whether the use of a
spill-treating agent is likely to achieve a net environmental benefit,
the Chief Conservation Officer must take into account

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assessment of the spill-treating agent's efficacy
  referred to in paragraph 11(4)(a);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the results of the analysis referred to in paragraph
  11(4)(b);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances referred to in paragraph 11(4)(c);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the methods and protocols referred to in paragraph
  11(4)(d);

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the monitoring plan referred to in paragraph
  11(4)(f); and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the results of any small-scale test conducted in
  respect of the agent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Small-scale test

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must, in respect of any small-scale test of a spill-treating
  agent referred to in section 166.1 of the Act, ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the test is conducted, the Chief
    Conservation Officer approves the carrying out of the test;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} during the test, the quantity of spill-treating
    agent applied is measured and recorded, the efficacy of the
    spill-treating agent is monitored and the factors that affect that
    efficacy are evaluated; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} after the test, the following information is
    submitted in writing, without delay, to the Chief Conservation
    Officer:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the volume of oil released and the volume
      treated,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of spill-treating agent that was
      used to conduct the test,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances under which the test was
      conducted, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the efficacy of the use of the spill-treating
      agent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following conditions must be met before a
  small-scale test is approved:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the operator must demonstrate that the quantity of
    spill-treating agent to be used in the test is the minimum required
    to evaluate the efficacy of its use; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a request to conduct an offshore
    subsurface test, the operator must demonstrate that, due to physical
    and environmental conditions, a surface test cannot be done or its
    efficacy cannot be readily determined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Net environmental benefit already
  determined

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No small-scale test is to be approved if the Chief
  Conservation Officer has made a determination for the purpose of
  section 166.1 of the Act regarding the net environmental benefit of
  the use of the spill-treating agent whose efficacy the test is
  intended to evaluate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oral or written approval

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Approval of a small-scale test may be provided orally
  or in writing but, if approval is provided orally, the Chief
  Conservation Officer must, as soon as the circumstances permit,
  provide to the operator written confirmation of the approval.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of approval

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Conservation Officer must vary the approval to use a
  spill-treating agent if new information indicates that a modification
  to the requirements set out in the approval is necessary to ensure
  that the approved use is likely to achieve a net environmental
  benefit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Conservation Officer must revoke the
  approval if new information indicates that, despite any modification,
  use of the agent will not likely achieve a net environmental benefit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of spill-treating agent

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any spill-treating agent is used in
  accordance with industry standards and best practices for
  spill-treating agent use, taking into account the local environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equipment and materials

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all equipment and
  materials that are listed in the contingency plan as required by
  paragraph 11(4)(e) are available and maintained in accordance with the
  manufacturers' specifications and ready for use at all times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring plan implementation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must implement the monitoring plan that
  is included in the contingency plan as required by paragraph 11(4)(f)
  at the commencement of the use of a spill-treating agent in the case
  of a spill.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to Chief Conservation
  Officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must inform the Chief Conservation
  Officer of the spill-treating agent's efficacy, the effects of its use
  on the environment and any changes that may require a modification to
  its use.

### [Well Abandonment, Suspension or Completion]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445557 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for suspension or abandonment

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator that suspends or abandons a well must ensure that the well

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} can be readily located; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is left in a condition such that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all petroleum-bearing pools and zones and
      discrete pressure zones are isolated, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any formation fluid is prevented from flowing
      through or escaping from the well-bore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification of isolation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before suspending or abandoning the well, the
  operator must verify the effectiveness of the isolations referred to
  in subparagraph (1)(b)(i) in accordance with the methods set out in
  its well approval application under paragraph 17(4)(e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional condition for suspension

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** An operator that suspends
a well must ensure that it is inspected and monitored to maintain its
integrity and prevent pollution.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional condition for abandonment

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** The operator of a well
must ensure that, on the abandonment of the well, the seabed is cleared
of any material or equipment that might have an adverse effect on the
marine environment or interfere with fishing activities or other uses of
the sea.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for drilling installation
removal

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** It is prohibited for the
operator of a drilling installation to remove the drilling installation
from a well or cause it to be removed unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the well has been abandoned, suspended or completed
  in accordance with these Regulations; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the removal of the drilling installation is for
  emergency purposes.

## [PART 9]{.HLabel1}[Diving Projects]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1445575 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessel used in diving project

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** An operator that conducts
a diving project must, in respect of a vessel used in the diving
project, ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is capable of providing the necessary dive
  support functions and operating safely;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is designed to withstand or avoid, without
  loss of its overall structural integrity or failure of its main safety
  functions, all foreseeable site-specific physical and environmental
  conditions or any foreseeable combination of those conditions;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is a [[Safety Convention
  vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 2 of the [Canada
  Shipping Act, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15), and holds a valid certificate
  of class issued by a classification society;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if a permanent diving system is installed on the
  vessel, the certificate of class referred to in paragraph (c) includes
  a valid class notation for diving issued by the classification society
  referred to in that paragraph; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a competent third party has assessed and certified
  the sea fastening of any equipment that is temporarily installed on
  the vessel for the diving project.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dynamic positioning system

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the dynamic positioning system on a vessel
  that is used in a diving project

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} includes safety-critical systems and components
    with sufficient segregation and redundancy to maintain the vessel's
    position in the event that credible scenarios of equipment failure,
    fire or flooding are realized;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes systems to monitor the parameters of
    critical system operability and the integrity of the dynamic
    positioning system and to provide alerts for critical system faults;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has sufficient redundancy to protect divers while
    diving;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is designed based on numerical analysis and model
    testing to ensure that the vessel's position reference and
    directional control can be maintained within specified tolerances
    that satisfy design operational requirements in relation to all
    functional loads and environmental loads to which the system may be
    subjected; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} is designed to ensure that, if the diving project
    involves saturation diving, the dynamic positioning system can
    withstand the loss from fire or flooding of all of its components
    situated in any one watertight compartment or fire subdivision of
    the vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After the design of the dynamic positioning system is
  completed, the operator must ensure that a failure modes and effects
  analysis is conducted to verify that the dynamic positioning system
  meets the requirements set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the dynamic positioning
  system is maintained so that it continues to perform in accordance
  with its design specifications.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Light dive craft

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator must ensure that any light dive craft that is used for a
  diving project is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fit for the purposes for which it is to be used;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed to withstand or avoid, without loss of its
    overall structural integrity or failure of its main safety
    functions, all foreseeable site-specific physical and environmental
    conditions or any foreseeable combination of those conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dive support vessel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, during all dives from a
  light dive craft, the availability of a dive support vessel that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is fitted with emergency equipment, including a
    fast rescue boat, that can provide assistance to the light dive
    craft in any foreseeable emergency situation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has a launch and recovery system for the light dive
    craft that has been verified and certified by the certifying
    authority as being fit for the purposes for which it is to be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[light dive
  craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[light dive
  craft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a small vessel or secondary craft
  that is equipped to deploy divers from a primary vessel.

## [PART 10]{.HLabel1}[Installations, Wells and Pipelines]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1445606 .Part}

### [Definitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445607 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Part.

[[air gap]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[air gap]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the clearance between the
    highest water or ice surface that occurs during extreme
    environmental conditions and the lowest exposed part of an
    installation not designed to withstand wave or ice
    impingement. ([tirant d'air]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[control station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[control station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a work area that is
    not continuously staffed that provides an alternative location to a
    control centre and the minimum necessary control equipment to enable
    essential management of the installation or of specific key
    systems. ([poste de contrôle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[damaged condition]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[damaged condition]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to a
    floating platform, the condition of the platform after it has
    suffered damage up to the extent determined in accordance with the
    applicable provisions of the MODU Code or, in the case of a platform
    that is not a mobile offshore drilling unit, the applicable rules of
    a classification society. ([état d'avarie]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[design service life]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[design service life]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the anticipated
    period during which any installation, including its systems or
    equipment, is to be used for its intended purpose, with anticipated
    maintenance but without substantial repair. ([vie
    utile]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hazardous area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
