:   [[hazardous area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area on an
    installation where flammable, explosive or combustible mixtures are
    or are likely to be present in sufficient quantities and for
    sufficient periods of time to require special precautions to be
    taken in the selection, installation or use of machinery and
    electrical equipment to prevent a fire or explosion. ([aire
    dangereuse]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[IS Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[IS Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the annex to International
    Maritime Organization Resolution MSC.267(85), International Code on
    Intact Stability, 2008. ([recueil IS]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[MODU Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[MODU Code]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the annex to International
    Maritime Organization Resolution A.1023(26), Code for the
    Construction and Equipment of Mobile Offshore Drilling Units,
    2009. ([Code MODU]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[process vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[process vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a heater, dehydrator,
    separator, treater or any other pressurized vessel used in the
    processing or treatment of produced petroleum. ([cuve de
    traitement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[unattended installation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[unattended installation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    installation on which persons are not normally present and in
    respect of which, when persons are present, it is for the purpose of
    performing operational duties, maintenance or inspections that will
    not require an overnight stay. ([installation non
    fréquentée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Installations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1445619 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1445620 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety and environmental protection

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that an installation, including its systems and equipment, is designed,
constructed, installed, arranged and commissioned so that it is fit for
the purposes for which it is to be used and can be operated safely
without posing a threat to persons or the environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design of installation

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
meeting the requirement under section 98 in respect of design, an
operator must ensure that an installation, including its systems and
equipment, is designed in accordance with the measures referred to in
clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(A) and 10(2)(b)(v)(A) that are described in the
operator's safety plan and environmental protection plan, respectively.

#### [Quality Assurance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1445625 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quality assurance program

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must, for the purpose of ensuring that an installation,
  including its systems and equipment, is fit for the purposes for which
  it is to be used, develop a quality assurance program that meets the
  following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it must be set out in writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it must be comprehensive;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it must include a process to achieve quality
    objectives and to comply with the requirements of these Regulations;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it must include the policies on which it is based
    and a process to communicate the policies to personnel and all other
    affected persons;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} it must set out the roles, responsibilities and
    authorities of all persons exercising functions under it, as well as
    the processes for making those persons aware of their roles,
    responsibilities and authorities and ensuring that they comply with
    them;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} it must include processes for establishing and
    maintaining measurable goals and performance indicators that are
    applicable to it;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} it must include processes for its periodic internal
    audit and review to identify areas for improvement and the
    corrective measures to be implemented if deficiencies are
    identified;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} it must include processes for ensuring that its
    integrity is preserved when changes to it are planned or
    implemented;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it must include processes for internal and external
    reporting on its performance; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} it must identify the resources that are necessary
    to ensure that the requirements under this section are being met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that each phase of the life
  cycle of the installation, from its design up to and including its
  decommissioning and abandonment, is carried out in accordance with the
  program and that any activity relating to the installation that is
  carried out under the control of a third party is also carried out in
  accordance with a quality assurance program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the processes and
  policies that are included in the program referred to in
  subsection (1) are readily accessible for consultation and
  examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organization

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the documentation
  relating to the program referred to in subsection (1) is organized and
  set out in a logical fashion to allow for ease of understanding and
  efficient implementation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Processes and procedures

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In this section, a reference to a process includes
  any procedures that are necessary to implement the process.

#### [Work Permits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1445647 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a work permit that is required under this
  Part is issued in either paper or electronic form, is approved by a
  person other than the one who issued it and sets out the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person who issued it and the person
    who approved it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of each person to whom it is issued;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the periods during which it is valid;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the work or activity to which it relates, the
    location at which the work or activity is to be carried out and any
    conditions to which the carrying out of the work or activity is
    subject; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any circumstances under which the work or activity
    is to be carried out that may have an effect on the safety and
    environmental risks associated with it, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} physical and environmental conditions,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any impediments to the proper use of any system
      or equipment, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any other activities being carried out in the
      area, with reference to the permit or certificate associated with
      those activities, if applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signatures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The work permit must bear the signatures of the
  person who issued it, the person who approved it and every person
  involved in the work or activity to which it relates, certifying that
  they have read and understood its contents.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operator obligations

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any work or activity that requires a work permit is
    done in accordance with the permit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any work permit that is issued is made readily
    accessible for the duration of the work or activity to which it
    relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention of copy

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must retain a copy of each work permit
  for at least three years after the day on which the work or activity
  to which it relates is completed.

#### [Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1445668 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Innovations

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any technology, including any technology
  that is used in relation to materials, design methods, joining
  techniques or construction techniques, that has not been previously
  used in comparable situations is not used in relation to an
  installation unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} engineering studies, prototypes or model tests
    demonstrate that the technology is safe and fit for the purposes for
    which it is to be used; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the technology is verified by a competent third
    party, in accordance with industry standards and best practices for
    technology qualification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technology qualification program

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must develop a technology qualification
  program that sets out the performance monitoring and inspection
  measures that are necessary to determine the effectiveness of any
  technology referred to in subsection (1) that it intends to use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program implementation and update

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the program is
  implemented and periodically updated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Physical and environmental conditions

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is designed to withstand or
  avoid all foreseeable site-specific physical and environmental
  conditions, or any foreseeable combination of those conditions,
  without compromising its structural integrity or that of any of its
  systems or equipment that are critical to safety or to the protection
  of the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the design of an
  installation is based on criteria that are determined using
  evidence-based regional and site-specific data, statistical analysis
  and modelling of physical and environmental conditions, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} oceanographic conditions, including any completely
    or partially submerged potential navigational hazards;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meteorological conditions, including the number of
    daylight hours;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} geotechnical conditions and geohazards;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} ice conditions and any other conditions associated
    with cold regions; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other physical and environmental conditions or
    naturally occurring phenomena that may adversely affect the
    installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ice conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that an installation that is
  to be operated where ice conditions may exist is designed and operated
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} minimize or avoid environmental loads associated
    with ice or ice and snow accumulation on the installation, including
    on its structural components;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the ice conditions will not adversely
    affect the functionality of any systems or equipment that are
    critical to safety or to the protection of the environment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} protect risers, offloading systems and other subsea
    systems from the ice conditions; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a mobile offshore platform or
    vessel,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prevent damage to propulsion or positioning
      systems from the ice conditions, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ensure safe transit through ice-infested waters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redundancy

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that there is redundancy
  included in any measures implemented for the purpose of paragraph
  (3)(a) in relation to ice and snow accumulation and removal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cold climate --- safety plan and
  environmental protection plan

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that an installation that is
  to be operated in a cold climate is designed, winterized and operated
  in accordance with the measures referred to in clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(B)
  and 10(2)(b)(v)(B) that are described in the operator's safety plan
  and environmental protection plan, respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cold climate --- design

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An installation that is to be operated in a cold
  climate must be designed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure the functionality in that climate of the
    installation and all of its systems and equipment that are critical
    to safety or to the protection of the environment, including in the
    case of property changes in fluids; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent any impact or damage to electrical cabling
    in open or unheated spaces and ensure that the cabling maintains its
    properties under cold-climate conditions.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design for intended use and location

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the structural components of an installation
  and any of its ancillary structures, including skids and modules, are
  designed for their intended use and location, taking into account

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the works and activities to be
    undertaken on and around the installation and the hazards associated
    with those works and activities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} material properties and dimensions of the
    installation that may vary over time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} failure modes; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} applicable safety factors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Analyses, tests, modelling and
  investigations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The design of the structural components of an
  installation and any of its ancillary structures, including skids and
  modules, must be based on any analyses, model tests, numerical
  modelling and site investigations that are necessary to determine the
  behaviour of the installation and of the soils that support it or its
  mooring systems under all foreseeable operating, construction,
  transportation and installation conditions --- including those
  involving geohazards --- and under all foreseeable loads during the
  design service life of the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design criteria

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The structural components of an installation and any
  of its ancillary structures, including skids and modules, must be
  designed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} withstand extreme loads that may occur during their
    construction and anticipated use;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} perform as intended during their operation under
    all anticipated normal loads;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} not fail under repeated loads;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} prevent damage that is disproportionate to the
    cause;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prevent localized damage from leading to
    progressive or complete loss of integrity of the structure;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} maintain structural integrity for the time
    necessary to safely evacuate all persons from the installation in
    the event of major damage caused by foreseeable hazards;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a floating platform,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} have sufficient stability and buoyancy reserve in
      the case of damage to ensure that credible scenarios of unintended
      flooding, if realized, do not result in the loss of the structure,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} incorporate sufficient redundancy in
      station-keeping systems to ensure that the structure can withstand
      the loss of a station-keeping component; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a self-elevating mobile offshore
    platform, withstand all loads to which the platform may be subjected
    in each mode of operation, including in the elevated position and
    during its removal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accidental loads

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (3)(d) to (f) and (h),
  the design must take into account all credible accidental load
  scenarios, including collisions between the installation and a vessel
  or aircraft.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for safe operation and
survival

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** Based on the results of
any analyses, tests, modelling or investigations undertaken under
subsection 105(2), the operator must ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} all physical and environmental conditions that could
  pose a hazard to the installation are documented and communicated to
  all affected personnel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the environmental limits for the safe operation of
  the installation are defined, included in operating procedures and
  communicated to all affected personnel; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} measures to detect, avoid, prevent, manage and reduce
  the effects of the hazards posed by the physical and environmental
  conditions are developed and implemented in operations and
  incorporated into the design of the installation where required.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk assessment --- fire, explosion and
hazardous gas

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an assessment of fire and explosion risks
  and of risks associated with hazardous gas and its containment is
  conducted in respect of an installation and that the assessment
  identifies

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the types of fires, explosions and hazardous gas
    releases that could occur, their potential sources and unmitigated
    consequences, the likelihood of their occurrence and, if applicable,
    their potential fire or blast loads;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} measures to be incorporated into the design of the
    installation, if practicable, to eliminate the hazards identified
    under paragraph (a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if it is not practicable to eliminate those hazards
    through design measures, all necessary control measures to reduce
    the risks associated with the hazards to a level that is as low as
    reasonably practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Elements for consideration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (1)(b) and (c), the
  assessment must take into account the following elements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the general layout of the installation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the production and process activities to be carried
    out, including well operations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} operating limits of the installation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the types of fires, explosions and hazardous gas
    releases identified under paragraph (1)(a) and their duration;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the need for a means of detecting, from the
    potential sources identified under paragraph (1)(a),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} hazardous gas releases, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} outbreaks of fire;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the need for a means of isolating and safely
    storing hazardous substances, including fuel, explosives and
    chemicals;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the need for a safe means of escape, evacuation and
    rescue in the event of a fire, explosion or hazardous gas release;
    and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the need for a means to ensure levels of emergency
    shutdown of the installation, systems and equipment in the event of
    the detection of a hazardous gas release or an outbreak of fire.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reliability and availability

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must demonstrate, through a risk and reliability analysis
  conducted using internationally recognized techniques, the reliability
  and availability of any system in an installation whose failure could
  cause or contribute to a major accidental event or whose purpose is to
  prevent or mitigate the effects of a major accidental event.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redundancies and measures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The risk and reliability analysis must determine the
  redundancies and measures that are required to protect a system
  referred to in subsection (1) from failure, including any redundancies
  and measures required under this Part for that system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Results of analysis

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the results of the risk
  and reliability analysis are reflected in the design of the
  installation, its systems and equipment and in any associated
  operating and maintenance manuals, including the operations manual
  referred to in section 157.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring program for physical and
environmental conditions

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must develop a monitoring program that involves the
  collection of data on physical and environmental conditions in
  sufficient quantities and at sufficient frequencies, and the retention
  of that data for sufficient periods, to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} support, during all works and activities, the
    identification of hazards and the assessment of the safety and
    environmental risks related to those hazards; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow for the timely implementation of control
    measures to address the identified risks and, if necessary, of the
    contingency plan referred to in section 11.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the operator must
  ensure that the installation is equipped to observe, measure and
  forecast physical and environmental conditions, to record data on
  those conditions and to obtain from external sources any additional
  data on those conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program implementation and update

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the monitoring program
  is implemented and periodically updated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Availability of data

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the data referred to in
  subsection (1) that may have an impact on safety and the protection of
  the environment is documented and provided to all persons that request
  it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection, monitoring, testing and
maintenance

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** An operator must, for
the purpose of facilitating the inspection, monitoring, testing and
maintenance of an installation, ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} relevant areas are clearly marked and identified;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is safe access to those areas;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an installation that is not intended
  to be periodically drydocked, there are means for carrying out an
  on-location inspection of the hull and underwater components;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} there is safe access to subsea equipment; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the installation is otherwise designed and equipped
  to permit those activities to be carried out.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Materials for installations

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the materials used in an installation are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fit for the purposes for which they are to be used
    and suitable for the conditions to which they may be subjected,
    including any foreseeable emergency situation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} non-combustible, unless essential properties are
    available only in materials that are combustible or the use of
    combustible material will not increase the risk to safety; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} selected to ensure that, in the case of fire or
    explosion, their use will not increase the risk to safety in the
    area of the fire or explosion or in adjacent areas, including by
    exposing persons to toxic fumes or smoke.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[non-combustible]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section,
  [[non-combustible]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of material,
  material that does not burn or give off flammable gases or vapours in
  sufficient quantity for self-ignition when heated to 750°C.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Passive fire and blast protection

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is designed and constructed
  with passive fire and blast protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design of passive fire protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The design of the passive fire protection must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not take into account the cooling effect from
    active firefighting equipment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take into account the need to inspect and maintain
    the passive fire protection components and the structures, divisions
    and equipment they are intended to protect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Divisions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the installation is
  divided such that spacing and barriers protect against accidental
  events and loads identified in the risk assessment conducted under
  subsection 107(1) or mitigate their effects.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barriers --- safety plan and
  environmental protection plan

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that barriers are designed,
  arranged, installed and maintained in accordance with the measures
  referred to in clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(C) and 10(2)(b)(v)(C) that are
  described in the operator's safety plan and environmental protection
  plan, respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barriers --- requirements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Barriers must be designed, arranged, installed and
  maintained to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contain fire, smoke, explosions and hazardous gas
    and prevent their effects from spreading into adjacent areas;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} protect persons from fire, smoke and explosions for
    the time necessary to enable them to escape to a temporary safe
    refuge;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} maintain for the necessary time, as determined on
    the basis of the safety studies referred to in section 116, the
    integrity of temporary safe refuges and of associated facilities
    that allow for communication, command, monitoring, control and
    evacuation against the effects of fire or explosion;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} protect safety-critical elements and equipment that
    are to remain operational in the event of an emergency from failure
    or malfunction caused by the effects of fire or explosion; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} maintain the installation's structural integrity
    against the effects of fire or explosion for the time necessary to
    safely evacuate all persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barriers --- level of protection

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The level of fire and blast protection that each
  barrier must provide is to be based on the results of the risk
  assessment conducted under subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barriers --- penetrations and openings

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A barrier must not have any penetrations or openings
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the penetration or opening is necessary for the
    functionality of the installation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the barrier is equipped to maintain its overall
    fire and blast integrity despite the penetration or opening; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there is a means of closing the penetration or
    opening, that means can be activated automatically or from outside
    the space being protected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barrier components

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that barrier components are
  certified by a competent third party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bulkheads --- production installation

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Unless the other combined features of a production
  installation can be demonstrated to provide at least the same level of
  protection, the operator must ensure that the following bulkheads are
  capable of preventing the passage of smoke and flame and of limiting
  the temperature rise on the unexposed face of the bulkhead to an
  average increase of 139°C and a maximum increase of 180°C above the
  initial temperature following 120 minutes of exposure to a hydrocarbon
  fire:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} those external bulkheads of the temporary safe
    refuges, main control centre, control stations, accommodations
    areas, embarkation stations and evacuation points, other than
    aircraft landing areas, that face production areas or wellheads; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the bulkheads that segregate the wellhead and
    processing areas from other areas of the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification society rules

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the passive fire and
  blast protection for an installation that does not hold a valid
  certificate of class issued by a classification society is at least
  equivalent to the protection required under the rules of a
  classification society for a mobile offshore drilling unit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hazardous and non-hazardous areas

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the boundaries between all hazardous areas
  and non-hazardous areas on an installation are delineated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification of hazardous areas

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, following the conduct
  of the risk assessment under subsection 107(1), each hazardous area is
  classified according to an internationally recognized, comprehensive
  and documented classification system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Separation of areas

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that hazardous areas of
  different classifications are separated from one another and from
  non-hazardous areas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direct access and openings

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, if practicable, that there
  is no direct access or other opening between hazardous areas and
  non-hazardous areas and between hazardous areas of different
  classifications or, if that is not practicable, that any direct access
  or opening between those areas is minimized and is designed to prevent
  uncontrolled air flow between them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Piping systems

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that piping systems are
  designed to ensure that there is no direct conduit between hazardous
  and non-hazardous areas and between hazardous areas of different
  classifications.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ventilation of enclosed hazardous areas

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any enclosed hazardous area on an
  installation is ventilated such that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} air is replaced at a rate sufficient to prevent
    hazardous gas accumulations in the enclosed hazardous area;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all air entering the enclosed hazardous area is
    from a non-hazardous area;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the air exhausted from the enclosed hazardous area
    does not increase the hazard level in another enclosed hazardous
    area or create a hazard in an enclosed non-hazardous area; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the ventilation system for the enclosed hazardous
    area is separate from the ventilation system for any non-hazardous
    area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mechanical ventilation system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a mechanical ventilation system is used for the
  purposes of subsection (1), the operator must ensure that the air in
  the enclosed hazardous area is maintained at a pressure that is lower
  than the pressure of any adjacent non-hazardous area or any adjacent
  hazardous area that is classified as less hazardous.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Air exhaustion from enclosed hazardous
  area

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all air exhausted from
  an enclosed hazardous area is vented to an outdoor area that, were it
  not for the vented air, would be a non-hazardous area or a hazardous
  area that would be classified as no more hazardous than the enclosed
  hazardous area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ventilation pressure differential and
  functionality

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that measuring devices are
  installed that will monitor any loss of ventilation pressure
  differential and any loss of functionality of each ventilation system
  for a hazardous area and that will, no more than 30 seconds after such
  a loss occurs, activate audible and visual alarms at the control
  points from which the system is monitored.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Positive overpressure relative to
  atmospheric pressure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must, in respect of the main control
  centre and all accommodations areas on an installation, ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are maintained at a positive overpressure
    relative to atmospheric pressure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all of their external doors that provide a primary
    means of access to them are equipped with airlocks; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all of their other external doors are equipped with
    airlocks or other means of maintaining and monitoring positive
    overpressure relative to atmospheric pressure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power shut-off for mechanical
  ventilation system

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the power source for a
  mechanical ventilation system that serves a hazardous area, a work
  area in a non-hazardous area or an accommodations area is capable of
  being shut off from the control station and from a position that is
  outside the area being ventilated and that will remain accessible
  during any fire that may occur within that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inlets and outlets of ventilation
  systems

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the main inlets and
  outlets of all ventilation systems are capable of being closed from a
  position that is outside the area being ventilated and that will
  remain accessible during any fire that may occur within that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ventilation system in non-hazardous
  area

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any ventilation system
  that serves a non-hazardous area is equipped with emergency devices in
  the event of a mechanical ventilation failure or the detection of
  hazardous gas, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} measuring devices to monitor any loss of
    ventilation pressure differential;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} audible and visual alarms;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an automated isolation device to prevent hazardous
    gas from entering the non-hazardous area; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a device to remotely seal the non-hazardous area
    --- including inlets and outlets of all ventilation systems --- from
    the control station and from a position outside the non-hazardous
    ventilated area that will remain accessible during any fire that may
    occur within the area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ignition prevention

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  order to prevent the ignition of flammable, combustible or explosive
  substances on an installation, an operator must ensure that measures
  are implemented to prevent the uncontrolled release or accumulation of
  those substances, including by ensuring that materials and equipment
  are properly arranged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design --- systems and equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any system or equipment
  that is to be used in a hazardous area is designed to control ignition
  sources and to prevent fire and explosions in that area, taking into
  account the area's classification under subsection 113(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk assessment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of meeting the requirements under
  subsections (1) and (2), the operator must ensure that any control
  measures identified in the risk assessment conducted under subsection
  107(1) are implemented.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other requirements --- equipment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any equipment located
  in a hazardous area is rated for use in that area and is installed,
  ventilated and maintained to ensure safe operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safe distance operation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any equipment that is
  operated in a non-hazardous area is operated at a safe distance from
  any flammable, combustible or explosive substances and is, unless it
  is rated for use in a hazardous area, equipped with an automatic and
  manual means of deactivation in the event of fire or hazardous gas
  detection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equipment in event of emergency

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any equipment that is
  located in a non-hazardous area and that is to remain in service in
  the event of an emergency associated with a gas release is rated for
  use in a hazardous area and is installed, ventilated and maintained to
  ensure safe operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cargo tank

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the gas mixture inside a cargo tank is maintained
    outside the explosive limits; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the systems associated with the cargo tank are
    designed to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prevent fire, gas or explosion hazards during all
      operating modes through the use of sufficient control measures,
      including alarms, and through redundancies in those measures, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ensure that affected persons are made aware when
      the systems become impaired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permit

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A work permit is required for all hot work carried
  out on an installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safe distances

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The work permit for hot work must set out safe
  distances to be maintained between the hot work and any well or any
  flammable, combustible or explosive substance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means of escape, evacuation and rescue

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that an installation is equipped with a safe means of escape, evacuation
and rescue, taking into account the results of the risk assessment
conducted under subsection 107(1) and comprehensive and documented
safety studies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary safe refuge

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator must ensure that the installation is equipped with a
  temporary safe refuge that will, in the case of an emergency,
  including an accidental event,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide sufficient space to accommodate all persons
    who may need to use the refuge until they have been evacuated, the
    accidental event has been brought under control or the emergency
    otherwise ends;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} protect the persons referred to in paragraph (a)
    from fire, gas release and explosion hazards for as long as they are
    in the refuge;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide the means for communication and command
    and, if applicable, for the monitoring and control of the accidental
    event for as long as persons are in the refuge; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide signage and lighting to enable safe
    evacuation from the refuge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Areas required to remain safe

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the accommodations
  area, main control centre and any other area of an installation that
  is required to remain safe for persons to occupy during an emergency,
  including the temporary safe refuge, are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designed to prevent ingress of hazardous
    substances; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed and located to enable occupation for the
    time required to implement emergency and evacuation procedures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periodic verification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must verify on a periodic basis that the
  temporary safe refuge meets the requirements set out in
  subsections (1) and (2) and must record the findings resulting from
  the verification.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exits, access and escape routes

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in any area where persons are normally present on
    an installation, there are at least two exits, each connected to an
    escape route that provides safe, direct and unobstructed access to
    temporary safe refuges, muster areas, embarkation stations and
    evacuation points; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there are means for persons to descend to the
    water.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(a), if an area referred to in
  that paragraph has an area less than 20 m^2^ or is a passage less than
  5 m in length, the operator must ensure that there is at least one
  exit as described in that paragraph in that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distancing --- exits

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the exits referred to
  in paragraph (1)(a) are separated as far apart from each other as
  possible to increase the likelihood that at least one exit and its
  connected escape route will be passable during an accidental event.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location of escape routes

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the installation has
  escape routes on two of its sides.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safe evacuation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all escape routes from
  an accommodations area or a temporary safe refuge to a muster area,
  embarkation station or evacuation point are clearly marked and
  illuminated and provided with fire protection to allow for the safe
  evacuation of persons in a time frame determined in the safety studies
  referred to in section 116.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Size of escape routes

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that each escape route is of
  sufficient size to enable the efficient movement of the maximum number
  of persons who may need to use it, as well as unrestricted manoeuvring
  of firefighting equipment and stretchers, taking into account the
  maximum number of persons who can be accommodated on the installation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Life-saving appliances for installation

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is equipped with life-saving
  appliances that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are sufficient in number and have the necessary
    redundancy to ensure their availability in any emergency situation;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} meet the requirements of the LSA Code and the annex
    to International Maritime Organization Resolution MSC.81(70),
    Revised Recommendation on Testing of Life-Saving Appliances, as if
    the installation were a vessel to which the Code and the Resolution
    apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loads

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that life-saving appliances
  can withstand all loads to which they may be subjected when they are
  in use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Space requirements and weight

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, in determining the
  number of persons any lifeboat, life raft or marine evacuation system
  can accommodate, the persons' space requirements and weight while
  wearing immersion suits are taken into account.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrangement and selection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the arrangement and
  selection of life-saving appliances are based on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safety studies referred to in section 116, in
    particular any escape and evacuation analysis that takes into
    account any major accidental events; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the results of the risk assessment conducted under
    subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Position

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that copies of a plan
  showing the position of all life-saving appliances are posted at the
  installation, including in the main control centre and in every
  accommodations area and work area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lifeboats --- availability

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsections (1) and (2), the
  operator must ensure, with respect to the lifeboats on an
  installation, that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are kept in at least two separate locations,
    one of which is adjacent to a temporary safe refuge;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have a combined capacity to accommodate the
    total number of persons on board the installation --- and those kept
    in each location have a combined capacity to accommodate the total
    number of persons assigned to that location --- even in the event
    that any one lifeboat is lost or rendered unusable; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the installation is a floating platform, those
    lifeboats that are able to be launched under any credible scenario
    of angle of heel have a combined capacity to accommodate the total
    number of persons on board the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lifeboats --- specifications

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the lifeboats are
  totally enclosed and are fire-protected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lifeboats --- continuous communication

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that each lifeboat is
  capable of being in continuous communication with each other lifeboat
  and with other vessels in the area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lifeboats --- towing devices

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that each lifeboat is
  equipped with towing devices.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Life rafts

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsections (1) and (2), the
  operator must ensure that the life rafts on an installation have a
  combined capacity to accommodate the total number of persons on board
  the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuous verification

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The operator must verify on a continual basis that
  the lifeboats, life rafts and other life-saving appliances are
  available and in a condition to perform as intended and must record
  the findings resulting from each verification.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Installation designed for removal

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is designed to facilitate
  its removal from the offshore area at the end of its design service
  life and to reduce any risks to safety, adverse effects on the marine
  environment and interference with navigation and other uses of the sea
  that may occur during and after its removal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the Board has
  approved, in the development plan, the abandonment or an alternative
  use of the installation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transportation and positioning

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation, or any part of it, is
  transported and positioned

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a manner that does not compromise safety or the
    protection of the environment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a manner that minimizes interference with and
    hazards to other activities in proximity to that installation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} under the supervision of a competent third party;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a self-elevating mobile offshore
    platform, with the legs of the platform secured in accordance with
    the rules of the classification society that issued the certificate
    of class required under section 140; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} with the support of vessels that are classified in
    accordance with section 177.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk assessment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before an installation, or any part of it, is
  transported and positioned, the operator must ensure that the
  following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a risk assessment must be conducted that takes into
    account

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} personnel requirements,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the towing vessels that will be used, the towing
      plan, including towing arrangements, and the operating limits of
      the towing equipment's components,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the processes and control measures to be
      implemented to ensure safety and the protection of the
      environment,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} physical and environmental conditions and the
      ability to reliably forecast those conditions, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any contingency measures to be taken in the event
      of adverse physical and environmental conditions or the occurrence
      of any other foreseeable adverse event during transportation and
      positioning; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a transportation and positioning plan must be
    prepared that takes into account any requirements of the competent
    third party referred to in paragraph (1)(c) and, if the installation
    is a floating platform, the plan must be prepared in accordance with
    the rules of the classification society that issued the certificate
    of class required under section 140.

#### [Systems and Equipment: Design, Installation, Commission and Other Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1445960 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electrical system

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any electrical system on an installation is
  designed to avoid any abnormal conditions and faults that may endanger
  the installation or, if it is not possible to avoid them, to provide
  alerts of those conditions and faults and mitigate their effects.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety and reliability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all electric motors,
  lighting fixtures, electrical wiring and other electrical equipment on
  an installation are safe and reliable under all foreseeable operating
  conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Device for monitoring insulation level
  to earth

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a primary or secondary distribution system for
  electrical power, heating or lighting with no connection to earth is
  used on an installation, the operator must ensure that the system is
  equipped with a device that continuously monitors the insulation level
  to earth and produces an audible or visual alarm to indicate
  abnormally low insulation values.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Main electrical power supply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the main electrical
  power supply on, or to, an installation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensures continuous availability of power generation
    and distribution;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes at least two power plants or other power
    supply sources, not including emergency power plants;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is capable of supporting all normal operations
    without recourse to the emergency electrical power supply required
    under subsection 126(1); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is capable of supporting all operations, other than
    drilling and production, if one of the power plants is out of
    operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Primary circuit shutdown

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the primary circuits
  from a power plant serving an installation are capable of being shut
  down from at least two separate locations, one of which must be the
  site of the power plant.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control system

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a control system is designed in accordance
  with the measures referred to in clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(D) and
  10(2)(b)(v)(D) that are described in the operator's safety plan and
  environmental protection plan, respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the control system is
  designed to meet the following requirements, taking into account human
  factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} controlled equipment must not be capable of being
    inadvertently activated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} controlled equipment must not create a safety or
    environmental hazard in the event of system failure or shutdown;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the system must have basic diagnostic capability;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the system must be capable of being operated
    simultaneously from multiple control stations without compromising
    safety.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of hardware

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that control system hardware
  is protected from circumstances, including excessive vibration, high
  electromagnetic field levels, electrical power disturbances and
  extreme temperatures or humidity levels or other physical and
  environmental conditions, that could cause mechanical damage to or
  degradation of the hardware or that could otherwise adversely affect
  the performance of the system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wireless remote control system

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any wireless remote
  control system includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a means for error checking to prevent the
    controlled equipment from responding to corrupt data; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a means for identification coding to prevent a
    transmitter other than the designated transmitter from operating the
    equipment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative means of control

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all control system
  functions that are required to ensure safety and are dependent on
  wireless communication links have an alternative means of control that
  can be activated without delay and without modification to the control
  system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection and testing

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Equipment that is to be operated by a new, repaired
  or modified control system must not be put into operation until the
  operator ensures that the control system has been inspected and tested
  to confirm that it functions as intended.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that documentation
  containing an up-to-date description of the design, installation,
  operation and maintenance of the control systems is readily accessible
  for consultation and examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Integrated software-dependent control
system

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an integrated software-dependent control
  system whose failure or malfunction would cause a hazard to safety or
  the environment is maintained to ensure its reliability, availability
  and security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control measures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that control measures are
  implemented to protect the integrated software-dependent system from
  any threat, including unauthorized access.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety-critical software

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator must ensure that any software that is a safety-critical
  element is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} secure, reliable and capable of being updated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed, commissioned and updated by competent
    persons; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} demonstrated to be fit for the purposes for which
    it is to be used through a testing and validation process that takes
    into account

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all foreseeable operating conditions and
      emergency situations, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} system complexity, dependencies and interactions
      between systems, software failure modes and the level of risk
      associated with system failure or malfunction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modification to features

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that no modification to the
  features of the software is implemented unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the modified software has undergone the testing and
    validation process referred to in paragraph (1)(c); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the necessary internal approvals for the
    modification have been obtained, including the approval of the
    installation manager.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency electrical power supply

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation has an emergency electrical
  power supply that is independent of the main electrical power supply
  such that the following systems and equipment continue to function in
  the event of a failure of the main electrical power supply:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} lights at

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all embarkation and debarkation stations and
      evacuation points,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all escape routes, temporary safe refuges,
      service corridors, accommodations area corridors, stairways, exits
      and personnel lift cars,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all control centres, control stations and areas
      from which the communication system referred to in section 129 is
      controlled,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} spaces from which drilling or production
      equipment, including any equipment that is critical to that
      equipment's operation, is controlled,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} spaces where equipment that is related to the
      emergency shutdown system referred to in section 133 and to the
      power plants referred to in paragraph 122(4)(b) is located,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} areas where emergency response equipment is
      stored, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} aircraft landing areas and the location of any
      obstacle to take-off and landing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} hazard detection systems, including the central
    monitoring system referred to in section 169 and the fire and gas
    detection system referred to in section 132;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} emergency response and life-saving systems,
    including life-saving appliances that require electrical power;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the communication system referred to in section
    129;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the emergency shutdown system referred to in
    section 133;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the lights and sound-signalling appliances referred
    to in section 127;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a floating platform, the pumps and
    powered watertight doors and hatches that are necessary to stabilize
    the installation, having regard to the failure modes and effects
    analysis referred to in subsection 144(5);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a column-stabilized mobile offshore
    platform, the ballast systems referred to in section 144;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the systems and equipment that are necessary to
    safely suspend at any time drilling or production that is in
    progress, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} blowout prevention systems, including the blowout
      preventer referred to in subsection 68(5),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any disconnectable mooring system referred to in
      section 148,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any disconnect system referred to in section
      150, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} pumping systems; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any other system or equipment that requires
    electrical power and that is referred to in the operator's safety
    plan referred to in section 9 or its contingency plan referred to in
    section 11.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mechanically driven generator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the emergency electrical power supply is a
  mechanically driven generator, the operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the installation is equipped with a transitional
    source of electrical power, unless the generator will automatically
    start and supply the necessary power in less than 45 seconds from
    the time the main electrical power supply fails;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the installation is equipped with a self-contained
    battery system that is designed, on failure or shutdown of both the
    main electrical power supply and the emergency electrical power
    supply, to automatically supply sufficient power to operate

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a period of at least one hour, the lights
      that are located in any emergency exit route, any escape route,
      any space where equipment incorporating an internal combustion
      engine, gas turbine, electric motor, generator, pump or compressor
      is found, any control centre and any emergency assembly room and
      at every launching station of life-saving appliances,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a period of at least one hour, the
      communication system referred to in section 129 and the general
      alarm system referred to in section 130, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for a period of at least four days, the lights
      and sound-signalling appliances referred to in section 127; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the mechanically driven generator has redundancy in
    its starting capabilities and a dedicated fuel source.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design and maintenance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the emergency
  electrical power supply together with any transitional source of
  electrical power and self-contained battery system with which the
  installation may be equipped are designed and maintained such that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are able to provide the systems and equipment
    referred to in subsection (1) with an emergency power supply of
    sufficient capacity, taking into account starting currents and the
    transitory nature of electrical loads, and sufficient duration to
    ensure that the systems and equipment can function as intended and
    to allow for effective management of the installation during an
    emergency, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to allow for the complete shutdown and evacuation
      of the installation,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to facilitate emergency response and the safe
      escape, refuge and evacuation of persons or to maintain the
      integrity of the installation,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to ensure sufficient power so that systems that
      must operate simultaneously can do so,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a floating platform, to maintain
      the flotation and stability of the platform, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} to bring a well to a safe state and to maintain
      it in that state;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} their capacity to provide power to essential
    systems is not compromised during their maintenance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they have sufficient redundancy to ensure their
    reliability and, as far as is practicable, to ensure their
    functional and physical independence from other essential systems
    or, if that is not practicable, they are arranged so as not to
    adversely affect or be adversely affected by the operation of those
    systems; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} they are readily accessible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection from damage

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the emergency
  electrical power supply, transitional source of electrical power and
  self-contained battery system referred to in subsection (3) are
  arranged --- or are otherwise protected from mechanical damage and
  damage caused by fire, explosion and physical and environmental
  conditions to which they may be exposed --- so that they remain
  capable of fulfilling their intended functions under all foreseeable
  operating conditions, including, in the case of a floating platform,
  under the static and dynamic angles of inclination referred to in
  subsection 136(7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alert

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, in the event of a
  failure of the main electrical power supply, all control centres are
  alerted by means of an audible and visual signal that the installation
  is being powered by the emergency electrical power supply.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lights and sound-signalling appliances

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that an installation is equipped with the lights and sound-signalling
appliances that are required by the [Collision
Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1416) as if that installation
were a Canadian vessel to which those Regulations apply, unless
compliance with the height and distance requirements of those
Regulations is not possible, in which case the lights and appliances
must be installed to maximize their audible and visual alerting
capabilities for collision avoidance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Radar

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that an installation other than an unattended installation is equipped
with radar for identifying hazards in proximity to the installation and
that the radar is continuously monitored.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communication system

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is equipped with a
  communication system that has built-in redundancy and is capable of
  communicating continuously, including in an emergency, with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} external emergency response teams;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all persons, individually or collectively, at an
    operations site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all persons who are in transit to or from an
    operations site;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all support craft;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} all onshore support centres;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} nearby vessels and aircraft; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} nearby installations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Radiocommunication system

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An operator must ensure that an installation other
  than an unattended installation is equipped with a radiocommunication
  system in respect of which the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the system must comply with Part 2 of the
    [Navigation Safety Regulations, 2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216)
    as if the installation were a Canadian vessel to which those
    Regulations apply;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a technical acceptance certificate must have been
    issued in respect of the system under the [Radiocommunication
    Act](/eng/acts/R-2); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a continuous listening watch and radio log must be
    maintained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Radiocommunication system ---
  unattended installation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An operator must ensure that any radiocommunication
  system on an unattended installation meets the requirements referred
  to in paragraphs (2)(a) and (b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General alarm system

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is equipped with a general
  alarm system that is capable of alerting persons on the installation
  of any hazards to safety or the environment other than fire or gas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the general alarm
  system is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} operational at all times other than when the system
    is being inspected, maintained or repaired;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} flagged as being subject to inspection, maintenance
    or repair, as required; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} designed to prevent tampering.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative means of alert

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a general alarm system is being inspected,
  maintained or repaired, the operator must ensure that there is an
  alternative means of alerting persons of the hazards referred to in
  subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gas release system

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation that includes process tanks,
  process vessels and piping is equipped with a gas release system that
  has a flaring system, a pressure relief system, a depressurizing
  system or a cold vent system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk assessment --- design

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the design of the gas
  release system is based on the results of the risk assessment
  conducted under subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the gas release system
  is designed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} release gas and combustible liquid from an
    installation in a controlled manner without creating a hazard to
    safety;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reduce pressure in the entire process system as
    quickly as possible while ensuring a safe and controlled release of
    pressure;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} minimize the effect on the environment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be activated from the main control centre and from
    control stations that meet the requirements set out in subsection
    (5); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} ensure that oxygen cannot enter the system during
    normal operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location --- system

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the gas release system
  is designed and located taking into account factors, including
  physical and environmental conditions, that affect the safe and normal
  flaring or emergency release of combustible liquid, gases or vapours
  so that when the system is in operation it does not damage the
  installation --- or any other installation, vessel or support craft in
  proximity to it --- or injure any person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control stations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the control stations
  from which the gas release system is activated are located and spaced
  so that they remain protected and accessible for safe operation of the
  system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Flaring systems

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must, in respect of any flaring system,
  ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if an unlit release of gas could produce toxic gas
    concentrations or gas concentrations of more than 50% of the lower
    explosive limit of the released gas,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the system has an automatic igniter system that
      has redundancy in its ignition capabilities, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an open flare system, the system
      and any associated equipment are designed to ensure a continuous
      flame; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the system and any associated equipment are
    designed to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} withstand the radiated heat at the maximum
      flaring rate,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} prevent flashback, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} withstand all loads to which they may be
      subjected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk minimization --- vents

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any vent that is used
  to release gas into the atmosphere without combustion is designed and
  located in accordance with the measures referred to in clause
  9(2)(b)(vi)(A) and subparagraph 10(2)(b)(vi) that are described in the
  operator's safety plan and environmental protection plan,
  respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liquid removal

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any liquid, other than
  water, that cannot be safely and reliably burned at the flare tip of a
  gas release system is removed from the gas before it enters the flare.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fire and gas detection system

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is equipped with a fire and
  gas detection system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire and gas
  detection system

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provides continuous, reliable and automatic
    monitoring functions to allow persons to be alerted to the presence
    and location of fire and hazardous gas, as well as the concentration
    and composition of that gas;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as far as is practicable, is functionally and
    physically independent of other essential systems or, if that is not
    practicable, is arranged so as not to adversely affect or be
    adversely affected by the operation of those systems;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} includes an alarm system with audible and visual
    alarms that are distinct from other types of alarms, that can be
    heard or seen at the main control centre and in other areas where
    persons are normally present, that are, on detection of fire or gas
    hazards, automatically activated and that can also be manually
    activated; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} allows control measures, including those that are
    designed to be initiated automatically, to be initiated manually to
    prevent abnormal conditions from escalating and causing major
    accidental events.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Risk assessment --- design

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the design of the fire
  and gas detection system is based on the results of the risk
  assessment conducted under subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire and gas
  detection system is designed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to detect the types of fire and hazardous gas
    releases identified in the risk assessment conducted under
    subsection 107(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to detect hazardous gas and smoke in the air
    intakes of any mechanically ventilated non-hazardous areas; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} such that the means to manually initiate fire and
    gas alarms are available at or near the office of the installation
    manager, at the main control centre, at every control station and at
    any other locations identified in the risk assessment conducted
    under subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire and gas
  detection system meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its detection components must

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be capable of detecting the types of fire and
      hazardous gas releases identified in the risk assessment conducted
      under subsection 107(1) in the areas in which they are located,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ensure reliable and early detection, taking into
      account their response characteristics, redundancy and performance
      under foreseeable conditions in which detection may be required,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} be rated and maintained for use in the areas in
      which they are located, as those areas are classified in
      accordance with the classification system referred to in
      subsection 113(2), and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} include failure and malfunction indicators;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the system and its components must be protected
    from mechanical damage and damage caused by fire, explosion and
    physical and environmental conditions to which they may be exposed
    so that they remain capable of fulfilling their intended functions
    under all foreseeable operating conditions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the system must allow for all necessary information
    to be continuously provided to the main control centre and other
    strategic locations to permit the management of emergency
    situations; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the system must be capable of being reset only if
    the cause of its activation has been resolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testing and maintenance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, in relation to the testing
  and maintenance of the fire and gas detection system, that the
  following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the system must be capable of being overridden for
    the purposes of testing and maintenance activities;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} override commands and functions must be applied for
    the shortest amount of time possible and with as few as possible
    being applied simultaneously; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the testing and maintenance activities must not
    impair the system beyond what is necessary to undertake those
    activities and must not impede its functioning.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permit

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A work permit is required for the testing and
  maintenance of the fire and gas detection system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management of override effects

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The work permit must set out measures to be taken to
  manage the effects of overriding the fire and gas detection system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leak repair

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any leak of gas that is
  detected by the fire and gas detection system or by means of an
  auditory, olfactory or visual method --- including the observation of
  the dripping of hydrocarbon liquids from an equipment component --- is
  repaired

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately, if the repair is necessary for the
    purposes of safety or the conservation of petroleum resources; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as soon as the circumstances permit, in any other
    case.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency shutdown system

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation has an emergency shutdown
  system that is capable of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shutting down all potential ignition sources and
    potential sources of flammable liquids or gases, including by
    isolating those sources;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} depressurizing all potential sources of flammable
    liquids or gases other than reservoirs;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} preventing abnormal conditions from escalating and
    causing major accidental events; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} limiting the extent and duration of any major
    accidental event.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Studies and assessments --- design

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the design of the
  emergency shutdown system is based on studies, analyses and
  assessments that identify potential hazards and must assess the risks
  associated with those hazards, including the risk assessment conducted
  under subsection 107(1) and the risk and reliability analysis referred
  to in section 108.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the emergency shutdown
  system is designed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} allow for automated and manual activation to ensure
    effective shutdown;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow for the shutdown of any system or equipment
    to bring it to a safe state, unless the system or equipment has been
    rated to remain operational in the area in which it is located, as
    that area is classified in accordance with the classification system
    referred to in subsection 113(2);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} allow for the selective shutdown of ventilation
    systems, other than fans that are necessary for supplying combustion
    air to engines that are required to operate during emergency
    situations unless gas has been detected in the intake to those
    engines;
