  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} allow for the isolation of petroleum and flammable
    fluid inventories, including reservoirs, wells, production systems
    and pipelines, from ignition sources;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} take into account the size and segregation of
    petroleum and flammable fluid inventories to limit the quantity of
    substances released on loss of containment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} allow for the depressurization and the disposal of
    hydrocarbon inventories in a safe manner and to a safe location
    without cold venting;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} allow for the closure of the installation's subsea
    and subsurface safety valves and of pipeline safety valves;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} take into account, in relation to all essential
    systems, the necessary timelines to support the safe escape, refuge
    and evacuation of persons and to maintain the integrity of the
    installation; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} take into account the activation of the fixed fire
    suppression systems required under paragraph 134(4)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shutdown logic

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the logic for the
  emergency shutdown system includes a hierarchy of shutdown levels,
  action sequences and timelines that are appropriate for the degree of
  risk posed by the hazards identified in the studies, analyses and
  assessments referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional requirements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, in relation to the
  emergency shutdown system, that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the system is reliable and, as far as is
    practicable, it is functionally and physically independent of other
    essential systems or, if that is not practicable, it is arranged so
    as not to adversely affect or be adversely affected by the operation
    of those systems;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the system includes an alarm system, with audible
    and visual alarms that are distinct from other types of alarms, that
    will automatically activate in the main control centre and at other
    strategic locations so that all affected persons, having regard to
    the hierarchy of shutdown levels referred to in subsection (4), are
    alerted to the emergency shutdown;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is continuous monitoring from the main
    control centre of the system's status, including, if the system or
    part of the system is overridden, the extent and duration of the
    override;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the system and its components are protected from
    mechanical damage and damage caused by fire, explosion and physical
    and environmental conditions to which they may be exposed so that
    they remain capable of fulfilling their intended functions under all
    foreseeable operating conditions;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the system allows for all information that is
    necessary to permit the management of emergency situations to be
    continuously provided to the main control centre and other strategic
    locations, including information regarding

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the shutdown level and the source of activation
      of the system,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any shutdown effects that failed to execute on
      activation of the system, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the status, including failure, of the system's
      components;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the system is capable of being activated from
    multiple manual activation points that are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} clearly marked,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} protected against unintentional activation, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} located at

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in the case of manual activation points for the
        highest level of shutdown, the main control centre and other
        strategic locations, including aircraft landing areas and other
        embarkation stations, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in the case of all other manual activation
        points, strategic positions, at least one of which must not be
        in a hazardous area;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the activation of the system from a manual
    activation point triggers the general alarm system referred to in
    section 130;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} if any part of the system is operated using a
    hydraulic or pneumatic accumulator,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the accumulator

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} is located as close as is practicable to the
        part that it is intended to operate, except if that part is part
        of a subsea production system, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} has the capacity for a sufficient number of
        activations to ensure that shutdown can be achieved, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the shutdown valves revert to a fail-safe mode
      in the event of a failure of the accumulator;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the system is capable of testing both its input and
    output signal devices and its internal functions to ensure its
    functioning;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} in the event of a failure of the main electrical
    power supply referred to in subsection 122(4), the system has the
    capacity to function continuously until the main electrical power
    supply is restored or all shutdown operations have been concluded;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} in the event that an impairment of the system or
    any of its components increases the risk to safety or the
    environment, any other systems that support the emergency shutdown
    system reverts to a fail-safe mode;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} if two or more installations are connected or if
    there is temporary equipment that has an emergency shutdown system
    on an installation,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the emergency shutdown systems of the connected
      installations are linked so that emergency shutdown signals are
      transmitted between those systems,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the emergency shutdown systems of the temporary
      equipment are linked to the installation's emergency shutdown
      system so that emergency shutdown signals are transmitted between
      those systems, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the logic for the emergency shutdown system of
      each of the connected installations and of the temporary equipment
      is re-evaluated and modified, if necessary, to take into account
      the fact that the emergency shutdown systems are linked, with the
      logic of the installation's emergency shutdown system being given
      priority over that of any temporary equipment;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} the system is capable of being overridden or reset
    only if the cause of its activation has been resolved and there has
    been local confirmation that the equipment that gave rise to the
    system shutdown can be safely used; and

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} override commands and functions are not capable of
    being unintentionally activated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testing and maintenance

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the emergency shutdown system is capable of being
  overridden for the purposes of testing and maintenance activities, the
  operator must ensure that the following requirements are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} override commands and functions must be applied for
    the shortest amount of time possible and with as few as possible
    being applied simultaneously; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the testing and maintenance activities must not
    impair the system beyond what is necessary to undertake those
    activities and must not impede the system's functioning.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work permit

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A work permit is required for the testing and
  maintenance of the emergency shutdown system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management of override effects

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The work permit must set out the measures to be taken
  to manage the effects of overriding the emergency shutdown system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Closure --- subsurface safety valve

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a production installation, the
  operator must ensure that, if the emergency shutdown system is
  activated, any subsurface safety valve closes not later than two
  minutes after the tree safety valve has closed unless a longer delay
  is justified by the mechanical or production characteristics of the
  well.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fire protection systems and equipment

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is equipped with fire
  protection systems and equipment to control and extinguish fires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire protection
  systems and equipment are designed, selected, operated, inspected,
  tested and maintained in accordance with the measures referred to in
  clause 9(2)(b)(vi)(B) that are described in the operator's safety
  plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design and selection

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The design and selection of fire protection systems
  and equipment, including suppression agents, must take into account
  their intended use and the results of the risk assessment conducted
  under subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further requirements

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire protection
  systems and equipment include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} automated fixed fire suppression systems that are
    capable of being manually activated from outside the space that is
    being protected;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fixed monitors, deluge systems and foam systems;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} manual firefighting systems and equipment; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any redundancies that are necessary to ensure that
    the systems function in the case of a failure of one of their
    components.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection from damage

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire protection
  systems and equipment are protected from mechanical damage and damage
  caused by fire, explosion and physical and environmental conditions to
  which they may be exposed so that they remain capable of fulfilling
  their intended functions under all foreseeable operating conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fixed fire suppression system

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that an automated fixed fire
  suppression system is installed in every accommodations area and
  hazardous area and in any other area that requires such a system based
  on the results of the risk assessment conducted under subsection
  107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fire pumps

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that at least two dedicated,
  segregated and independently driven fire pumps supply a dedicated
  firewater ring main and that each of those fire pumps is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} equipped with at least two independent starting
    devices; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designed to allow for both local and remote
    control.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire pumps are
  located as far as possible from equipment used for storing and
  processing petroleum, taking into account the results of the risk
  assessment conducted under subsection 107(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supply of firewater

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the fire pumps and
  piping and their valves are capable of providing a sufficient supply
  of firewater to any area on the installation, including if a segment
  of the firewater ring main is damaged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Firewater system

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the firewater system
  is capable of operating continuously for a minimum of 18 hours.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fire hydrants and hose reels

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the number and
  location of fire hydrants and fire hose reels are such that at least
  two jets of water, not emanating from the same location, can reach any
  part of the installation where a fire may occur.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Portable fire-extinguishing equipment

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} In areas where it is not practical to use fire
  hydrants and fire hose reels, the operator must ensure that portable
  fire-extinguishing equipment is readily available and accessible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alarms at main control centre

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that audible and visual
  alarms will activate at the main control centre on the initiation of
  any of the automated fixed fire suppression systems or on the loss of
  any firewater pressure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional alarms

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} If the automated fixed fire suppression system
  creates a hazard to persons, the operator must ensure that audible and
  visual alarms automatically activate inside and outside the space that
  is being protected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unattended installations

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs (4)(a) and (b) and subsections (6)
  to (11) do not apply in respect of unattended installations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Boilers and pressure systems

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that boilers and pressure systems are designed in
  accordance with the measures referred to in clause 9(2)(b)(vi)(C) that
  are described in the operator's safety plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The boilers and pressure systems must be designed to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prevent the occurrence of an abnormal condition
    that could cause an undesirable event;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent an undesirable event from causing a release
    of liquids, gases or vapours;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prevent the ignition of any flammable liquids,
    gases or vapours that are released;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} safely disperse or dispose of any liquids, gases or
    vapours that are released;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prevent the formation of explosive mixtures;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} limit persons' exposure to fire hazards;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} monitor safe limits of pressure, temperature and
    fluid levels and reliably protect against exceeding those limits;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} permit the examination of components critical to
    the pressure system to ensure their continued integrity;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} allow for draining and venting at all stages of
    operation to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} permit cleaning, inspection and maintenance
      activities to be carried out safely, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} avoid harmful effects, including water hammer,
      vacuum collapse, corrosion and uncontrolled chemical reactions;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} prevent the escalation in relation to the boilers
    and pressure systems of accidental events occurring outside of them;
    and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} limit and mitigate the effects of any loss of
    containment of the contents of the boilers and pressure systems.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The design of boilers and pressure systems must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be based on standards that incorporate safety
    margins, that conform to good engineering practice and that involve
    the carrying out of analyses and numerical modelling as necessary to
    determine the behaviour and failure modes of the boilers and
    pressure systems under all foreseeable operating conditions, taking
    into account

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the internal and external pressures to which the
      boilers and pressure systems are subjected,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ambient and operating temperatures,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} static pressure and the mass of the contents of
      the boilers and pressure systems when tested or operated,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} foreseeable dynamic loads and reaction forces
      and moments resulting from, among other things, piping and its
      supports and other accessories,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} structural and mechanical integrity threats, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} reactions caused by changes in fluids and other
      substances contained in the boilers and pressure systems over
      time, including reactions caused by the products of the
      decomposition of unstable fluids or substances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if hazards cannot be eliminated, incorporate safety
    measures that take into account

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the need for closing and opening devices and
      devices to indicate their status and to prevent their opening or
      physical access to them while pressure differential exists,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the need to contain hazardous substances and to
      mitigate the effects of any hazard related to their release,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the surface temperature of the boilers and
      pressure systems, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the decomposition of unstable fluids; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be approved by an authorized inspector.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loads and other factors

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that boilers and pressure
  systems can withstand all combinations of loads, pressures,
  temperatures, fluids and substances to which they may be subjected
  during their design service life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Materials used

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the materials used for
  the manufacture of boilers and pressure systems are compatible with
  their operating environment and are chemically resistant to the fluids
  they contain during their design service life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacturer's documents and records

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the following documents
  and records are obtained from the manufacturer of the boilers and
  pressure systems:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} documents demonstrating that manufacturing, testing
    and installation have been carried out in accordance with the design
    specifications provided for in a quality assurance program that is
    approved by an authorized inspector;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} records of the procedures that were followed in the
    welding, brazing and non-destructive examination of the boilers and
    pressure systems, including the results of the welder qualification
    tests specific to the welding and brazing procedures;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} documents evidencing the qualifications of persons
    involved in manufacturing, inspection and testing, including
    welders; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} traceability records for the components of the
    boilers and pressure systems.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Construction, installation,
  commissioning, inspection and testing

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, before a boiler or pressure
  system is put into operation, that it has been

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} constructed, installed and commissioned by persons
    with the necessary experience, training, qualifications and
    competence to do so safely and in a manner that protects the
    environment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subjected to any inspections by an authorized
    inspector and tests by or under the direction of an authorized
    inspector, including non-destructive examination and proof tests,
    that are necessary to ensure its integrity and compliance with
    design specifications.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized inspector

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a boiler or pressure
  system is inspected by an authorized inspector and tested by or under
  the direction of an authorized inspector

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the boiler or pressure system is put into
    operation following its installation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before the boiler or pressure system is put into
    operation following any modification or repair to it, including
    welding; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at any other interval as required by the standards
    on which the design of the boiler or pressure system is based.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operating procedures

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that operating procedures
  are developed for the boilers and pressure systems that inform users
  of operating hazards and indicate any special measures to be taken to
  reduce risks when the boilers and pressure systems are being used,
  maintained or repaired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conformity with procedures

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any boiler or pressure
  system is used, maintained and repaired in accordance with the
  operating procedures referred to in subsection (9).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alteration of fitting

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} It is prohibited for any person to alter, interfere
  with or render inoperative any boiler or pressure system fitting,
  except for the purpose of adjusting or testing the fitting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Register

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} The operator must keep a register of all boilers and
  pressure systems that includes the following documents and information
  in respect of each:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} accurate design calculations, technical drawings
    and design specifications, including evidence of the design approval
    by an authorized inspector;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a list of the standards on which the design of the
    boiler or pressure system is based;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the boiler or pressure system's operating limits,
    including its pressure and temperature ratings;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all documents and records required from the
    manufacturer under subsection (6);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each inspection and test referred to
    in subsection (7) or (8), a record created and signed by the
    authorized inspector who conducted the inspection that includes

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of the inspection or test,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} information that identifies the boiler or
      pressure system that was inspected or tested, as well as its
      location,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the range of safe pressure and temperature at
      which the boiler or pressure system may be operated,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a declaration by the authorized inspector who
      conducted the inspection or who conducted or directed the test as
      to whether the boiler or pressure system meets the standards that
      were applied in its design and manufacture,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a declaration by the authorized inspector who
      conducted the inspection or who conducted or directed the test
      stating that the boiler or pressure system is fit for the purposes
      for which it is to be used,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} any recommendations regarding the need for
      modifications to the maintenance program established under section
      159, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} any other observation relevant to safety; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a description of each repair or modification made
    to the boiler or pressure system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marking

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a boiler or pressure
  system is marked with any information that is necessary for its safe
  installation and operation, including an identifier that permits
  reference to the documents and records referred to in subsection (6)
  and the information referred to in paragraphs (12)(e) and (f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all operating
  procedures developed in accordance with subsection (9) and the
  register referred to in subsection (12) are periodically verified by
  the certifying authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to any of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a heating boiler that has a heating surface of 3
    m^2^ or less;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a pressure system that is installed for use at a
    pressure of one atmosphere of pressure or less;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a pressure vessel that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has a capacity of 40 L or less, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has an internal diameter of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} 152 mm or less, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} more than 152 mm but not more than 610 mm if
        the pressure vessel is used for the storage of hot water or is
        connected to a water pumping system containing compressed air
        that serves as a cushion;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a refrigeration plant that has a refrigeration
    capacity of 18 kW or less; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a domestic water and plumbing system.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mechanical equipment

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any mechanical equipment on an installation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is designed, selected, located, installed,
    commissioned, protected, operated, inspected and maintained in
    accordance with the measures referred to in clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(E)
    and 10(2)(b)(v)(E) that are described in the operator's safety plan
    and environmental protection plan, respectively; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} can operate safely and reliably under all
    foreseeable operating conditions, taking into account the
    manufacturer's instructions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Mechanical equipment must be designed to eliminate
  hazards to safety or the environment in the following scenarios or, if
  that is not possible, to mitigate the risks posed by those hazards:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} loss of containment of hazardous substances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} overspeeding and loss of restraint of machinery
    components with high kinetic energy;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} extreme surface temperatures of the mechanical
    equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} movement of mobile components of the mechanical
    equipment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} loss of control and integrity of the mechanical
    equipment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} ignition of potentially explosive atmospheres in
    hazardous areas from sparks, flames or excessive heat; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} escalation of accidental events.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Controls and manual shut-off devices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that controls and manual
  shut-off devices for mechanical equipment are in a protected and
  readily accessible location that permits safe operation when an
  accidental event occurs that renders the equipment inaccessible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Internal combustion engine ---
  operating instructions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the basic operating
  instructions for an internal combustion engine provide details of
  stop, start and emergency procedures and are permanently attached to
  the engine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Turbines and internal combustion
  engines

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that turbines and internal
  combustion engines are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} equipped to prevent unintended ignition;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} installed so that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} their supply of combustion air is from a
      non-hazardous area, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} their exhaust is discharged to a non-hazardous
      area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} equipped with safety devices --- including manual
    fuel shut-off devices and, unless it would increase safety or
    environmental risks, automatic fuel shut-off devices --- to prevent
    major damage from overspeeding, high exhaust temperature, high
    cooling water temperature, low lubricating oil pressure or other
    foreseeable hazards that could impair the safety of operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (5)(c), turbines and internal
  combustion engines that are critical to emergency response, including
  emergency generators and fire pumps, need only be equipped with safety
  devices to prevent major damage from overspeeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operation of critical mechanical
  equipment

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that mechanical equipment
  that is critical to the safety or propulsion of a floating platform
  will continue to operate safely and reliably at its full rated power
  under the static and dynamic angles of inclination that are specified
  in the rules of the classification society that issued the certificate
  of class required under section 140.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Materials handling equipment

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that all materials handling equipment is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to the extent feasible, designed and constructed to
    prevent the failure of any of its parts, taking into account the
    conditions under which it is to be operated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the extent feasible, equipped with safety
    devices that will ensure that any failure of any of its parts does
    not result in a loss of control of the equipment or of its load or
    result in any other hazardous situation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} operated taking into account the manufacturer's
    instructions and industry standards and best practices.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all materials handling
  equipment is marked with its rated capacity and in a manner that
  identifies its manufacturer and model and that permits reference to
  any information that is necessary to its safe operation, including
  information regarding its design, construction, inspection, testing,
  maintenance and repair.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection and proof test

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that materials handling
  equipment that is to be used on an installation is inspected and
  proof-tested by a competent third party in the following situations to
  determine the equipment's rated capacity:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the equipment is to be used on the installation for
    the first time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} repairs or modifications have been made to the
    equipment's load-bearing components;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the equipment has been in contact with an electric
    arc or current; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} there is any other reason to doubt that the rated
    capacity of the equipment that was most recently certified under
    subsection (5) or the limitations that were most recently indicated
    under that subsection continue to be accurate, including as a result
    of damage sustained by the equipment or modifications made to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria for inspection and testing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the inspection and
  proof-testing is done in accordance with criteria established by the
  manufacturer or applicable industry design and safety standards,
  including with respect to the frequency at which the equipment must be
  inspected and proof-tested to ensure its continued safe operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rated capacity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Following the inspection and proof test, the
  competent third party must certify in writing the rated capacity of
  the materials handling equipment and must indicate in writing any
  limitations that must be imposed on its use having regard to physical
  and environmental conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency slewing and lowering

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a crane with slewing
  capability is capable of retaining its slewing and lowering capability
  in emergency situations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pedestal crane

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a pedestal crane meets
  the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it must be equipped with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} appropriate travel-limiting devices for its boom,
      hoist, blocks and slewing mechanism,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a load-measuring device that has been calibrated
      in accordance with the manufacturer's specifications or any
      calibration standard that is at least as rigorous as those
      specifications,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a device to indicate its boom extension or load
      radius, if its rated capacity is affected by the extension or
      radius,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a device to indicate its boom angle, if its
      rated capacity is affected by that angle,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a device for accessing anemometer readings, if
      the load that it is able to safely handle or support is
      susceptible to being reduced by wind,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a gross overload protection system, if it is
      used to move persons or things to or from a floating platform or
      vessel, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a safe load indicator system that is programmed
      for different operating modes and includes load and moment
      measuring devices; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a load chart that specifies the boom angle and the
    safe working load for each block and for each operating mode, as
    well as any limitations indicated under subsection (5), must be
    posted inside its control cab.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Crane hooks

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all crane hooks are
  equipped with spring-loaded latches or other equally effective means
  of preventing the load from falling off the hook under any operating
  conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Landing or take-off

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} When an aircraft is landing on or taking off from a
  landing area, it is prohibited to move a crane in the vicinity of the
  landing area and, if feasible, the person operating the crane must
  ensure that the crane's boom is stowed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lifting device certification

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any materials handling
  equipment that lifts over 10 tonnes is certified by the certifying
  authority.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsea production system

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a subsea production system is designed,
  constructed, installed, commissioned, operated, inspected, monitored,
  tested and maintained in accordance with the measures referred to in
  clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(F) and 10(2)(b)(v)(F) that are described in the
  operator's safety plan and environmental protection plan,
  respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Design

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A subsea production system must be designed so that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the system can avoid foreseeable hazards or revert
    to a safe state when hazards are imminent;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the system supports and seals connections to the
    well, pipelines, other subsea production systems or other
    installations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the event of a loss of control or communication,
    the system will revert to a safe state;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the failure of a single component of the system
    cannot cause or contribute to a major accidental event;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} barrier elements in each conduit that carries
    fluids are reliable, have the necessary redundancy and are arranged
    to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prevent uncontrolled flow of well fluids,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} minimize the quantity of fluids released from
      the conduit in the event of unintended release, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} permit testing of the integrity of the barrier
      elements without increasing safety or environmental risks;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} subsea equipment can withstand or is protected from
    any load to which it may be subjected that would result in
    mechanical damage;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} production risers can withstand or are protected
    from all hazards and environmental loads to which they may be
    subjected, other than icebergs; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the blowout preventer is supported by the system
    during drilling and the tree and any workover or intervention
    pressure control equipment are supported by the system after
    completion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disconnectable riser

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a riser that is
  connected to a floating platform that has a disconnectable mooring
  system or dynamic positioning system is designed to be capable of
  safely detaching in any foreseeable physical and environmental
  conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Riser disconnect

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, if risers are designed
  to disconnect in order to avoid foreseeable hazards, riser fluids may
  be safely displaced by water or isolated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Riser integrity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that, if a riser is
  disconnected, its integrity is demonstrated through testing once it is
  reconnected and before it is brought back into service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control of subsea production system

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a subsea production
  system is controlled from only one location at any given time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure modes and effects analysis

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any subsea production
  system is assessed through a failure modes and effects analysis.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary or portable equipment

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that any temporary or portable equipment used on
  an installation is fit for the purposes for which it is to be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of temporary or portable
  equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before any temporary or portable equipment is
  installed or brought into service on an installation, the operator
  must ensure that the equipment and its integration with other
  equipment and systems are assessed to determine their impact on
  safety-critical elements and on the risk assessment referred to in
  subsection 24(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that temporary or portable
  equipment is managed in accordance with the measures referred to in
  clauses 9(2)(b)(v)(G) and 10(2)(b)(v)(G) that are described in the
  operator's safety plan and environmental protection plan,
  respectively, and in a manner that does not compromise the target
  levels of safety set out in those plans.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification by certifying authority

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that temporary or portable
  equipment that is a safety-critical element is, before being put into
  operation, verified by the certifying authority to confirm its
  suitability and safe placement and hook-up.

#### [Additional Requirements for Platforms]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1446449 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a floating platform holds a valid certificate of class issued by a
classification society that corresponds to the authorized work or
activity to be carried out from the floating platform.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Air gap

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a platform that is either founded on the seabed or
column-stabilized has a sufficient air gap to operate safely under the
maximum environmental load conditions to which it may be subjected.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stability

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a floating platform, whether intact or in a
  damaged condition, is stable and can be operated safely, having regard
  to all motions and loads to which it may be subjected, including by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determining the stability and motion response
    characteristics of the platform using analysis or model testing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determining the critical maximum loads and motions
    that the platform can withstand;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensuring that all equipment is fastened to prevent
    unintended movement; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} monitoring and recording all loads that could
    affect the motions, stability or inclination of the platform.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Freeboard

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a floating platform has
  sufficient freeboard to operate safely under the maximum environmental
  load conditions to which it may be subjected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement --- Codes

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must comply with the applicable
  provisions of the MODU Code and Part B of the IS Code concerning the
  stability and motion response of a floating platform, which are to be
  read as mandatory.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadweight survey

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the weight of a floating platform or a
  self-elevating mobile offshore platform changes by more than 1% of the
  lightship weight, the operator must ensure that a deadweight survey is
  carried out at the earliest opportunity and an up-to-date value of the
  lightship centre of gravity is calculated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Self-elevating mobile offshore platform

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must, in relation to a self-elevating mobile offshore
  platform, ensure that a site-specific assessment is conducted of the
  condition of the seabed, including seabed restraint, to ensure that
  the platform is stable and can be operated safely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a self-elevating mobile
  offshore platform meets the following requirements:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it must be equipped with systems to actively
    monitor

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} hull inclination,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} leg penetration into the seabed,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} loads on each of the platform's legs, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} rack phase differential, if applicable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its jacking mechanisms must be designed so that the
    failure of a single component does not cause an uncontrolled descent
    of the platform.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of operations and well
  shut-in

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the works and
  activities on a self-elevating mobile offshore platform are suspended
  and that all wells associated with the platform are brought to a safe
  shut-in condition if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hull inclination or the rack phase differential
    exceeds the allowable limits set out in the operations manual in
    accordance with paragraph 157(3)(b);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unexplained changes occur in the loads on any of
    the platform's legs;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} leg penetration into the seabed increases; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other event threatens the stability of the
    platform.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective measures

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of any of the situations referred to in
  subsection (3), the operator must ensure that the works and activities
  on the self-elevating mobile offshore platform remain suspended and
  that all wells associated with the platform remain in a safe shut-in
  condition until the cause of the situation has been investigated and
  corrective measures have been taken.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ballast and bilge systems

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a floating platform is equipped with
  reliable ballast and bilge systems with the necessary redundancy in
  their components to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} maintain necessary draught, stability and hull
    strength under all foreseeable operating conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} return the floating platform to a safe condition
    from an unintended draught, trim or heel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prevent unintended transfer of fluid within the
    system;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} empty and fill all tanks that are a part of the
    system; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} completely and rapidly empty watertight spaces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement --- Code

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must comply with the applicable
  provisions of the MODU Code concerning ballast and bilge systems,
  which are to be read as mandatory.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secondary ballast control station

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a column-stabilized mobile offshore
  platform, the operator must ensure that it is equipped with a
  secondary ballast control station that is equipped with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an effective means of communication with other
    spaces that contain equipment relating to the operation of the
    ballast system;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a ballast pump control and status system;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a ballast valve control and status system;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a tank level indicating system;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a permanently mounted ballast schematic diagram;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} heel and trim indicators;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a draught-indicating system;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a system to indicate the available power from the
    main and emergency electrical power supplies; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a ballast system hydraulic or pneumatic
    pressure-indicating system.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location --- secondary ballast control
  station

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a secondary ballast
  control station is located above the waterline in the final condition
  of equilibrium after flooding if the floating platform is in a damaged
  condition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure modes and effects analysis

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the ballast and bilge
  systems are assessed through a failure modes and effects analysis
  before any authorized work or activity is carried out from the
  floating platform.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Watertight and weathertight integrity and
freeboard

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operator must comply with the applicable provisions of the MODU Code
  and Part B of the IS Code concerning watertight and weathertight
  integrity and freeboard, which are to be read as mandatory.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Watertight subdivision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the floating platform
  is designed with sufficient watertight subdivision to ensure the
  preservation of reserve buoyancy and damage stability under all
  foreseeable conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Load line certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a floating platform

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} holds an International Load Line Certificate or an
    International Load Line Exemption Certificate issued by the
    government of the state whose flag the platform is entitled to fly,
    as required under Article 16 of the International Convention on Load
    Lines, 1966; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is marked in accordance with the certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Watertight and weathertight appliances

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the arrangement and
  specification of watertight and weathertight appliances complies with
  the measures referred to in clause 9(2)(b)(v)(H) that are described in
  the operator's safety plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Water ingress

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a floating platform is
  designed with systems and equipment that provide for the operation,
  monitoring and indication --- both locally and at the ballast control
  stations --- of the opening and closing of watertight doors and
  hatches and for the detection and provision of alerts of any water
  ingress into watertight spaces that are not designed to accumulate
  liquid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Port lights

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the columns of a
  column-stabilized mobile offshore platform do not have port lights or
  similar openings.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Station-keeping

**[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a floating platform is equipped with a mooring system or a dynamic
positioning system to ensure station-keeping of the platform within its
operating limits.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mooring system

- **[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a mooring system with which a floating
  platform is equipped is designed, on the basis of analysis and model
  testing, to ensure

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} safety and the protection of the environment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the stability and serviceability of the floating
    platform;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the integrity and serviceability of the mooring
    system components, including any related topside equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the integrity and serviceability of drilling
    risers, production risers, export risers or any other type of riser;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the necessary redundancy of the mooring system
    components to enable the floating platform to maintain its position
    with the loss of a single component or, for a thruster-assisted
    mooring system, the loss of the most effective thruster or a single
    failure in the power or control system;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for a thruster-assisted mooring system, the ability
    of the floating platform to withstand extreme meteorological
    conditions in the event of a power failure;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the ability of the floating platform to move from
    its position to avoid accidental events that it is not designed to
    withstand; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} safe access and safe clearances with respect to
    subsea and surface components of the installation, any nearby
    installations, support vessels and evacuation systems.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excursion limits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the excursion limits of
  a floating platform that is equipped with a mooring system are
  established on the basis of the analysis and model testing referred to
  in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of station-keeping or failure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the floating platform
  has systems and processes to continuously detect loss of
  station-keeping or the failure of any mooring system component.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that mooring line tensions
  or other indicators of the integrity of the mooring system are
  monitored and kept within the mooring system's operating limits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that measures to ensure that
  the mooring system continues to perform in accordance with its design
  specifications are implemented, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assessment of the system's condition,
    periodically and if it is damaged or if damage to it is suspected;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the making of arrangements for timely repair or
    replacement in the event of damage or deterioration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disconnectable mooring system

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the mooring system with which a floating platform is equipped is
  disconnectable, the operator must ensure that the system is designed
  to ensure that disconnection can be accomplished in a controlled
  manner without creating a risk of drift-off.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the disconnectable
  mooring system is designed and maintained in accordance with the
  measures referred to in clause 9(2)(b)(vi)(D) that are described in
  the operator's safety plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Primary and backup systems

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the disconnectable
  mooring system includes a primary system and a backup system for
  disconnection, both of which can be operated locally or from a remote
  location.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Floating platform capability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a floating platform
  that is equipped with a disconnectable mooring system is capable of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} safely manoeuvering away under its own power; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} maintaining a safe position and heading while
    disconnected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria and procedures for
  disconnection

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that criteria and procedures
  for disconnection are developed for all credible disconnection
  scenarios, including procedures for monitoring environmental
  conditions and providing alerts for worsening conditions that may
  require disconnection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disconnection and reconnection

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the disconnectable
  mooring system

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is capable of carrying out a planned disconnection
    after allowing time for the depressurization and flushing of subsea
    flowlines;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is capable of carrying out an emergency
    disconnection after allowing time to safely shut in wells and subsea
    equipment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} allows for the reconnection to the floating
    platform of the system and flowlines in an orderly sequence, in the
    physical and environmental conditions described in the operations
    manual under paragraph 157(2)(c); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} allows for the resumption of production after the
    system and flowlines have been reconnected to the floating platform
    following a planned disconnection;

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periodic verification of disconnection
  capability

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The operator must periodically verify the disconnect
  capability of the disconnectable mooring system and must record the
  findings resulting from the verification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excursion limits exceeded

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the emergency
  disconnection referred to in paragraph (6)(b) is initiated if the
  floating platform exceeds the excursion limits established under
  subsection 147(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dynamic positioning system

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the design of a dynamic positioning system
  with which a floating platform is equipped

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is based on numerical analysis and model testing to
    ensure that the floating platform's position reference and
    directional control can be maintained within specified tolerances
    that satisfy design operational requirements in relation to all
    functional and environmental loads to which the system may be
    subjected at the floating platform's intended location;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is based on a failure modes and effects analysis to
    ensure the segregation and redundancy of safety-critical systems and
    their components as necessary to maintain the platform's position in
    the event that credible scenarios of equipment failure are realized;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} allows the dynamic positioning system to withstand
    the loss from fire or flooding of all of its components situated in
    any one watertight compartment or fire subdivision of the floating
    platform; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} includes systems to monitor the parameters of
    operability and integrity of the critical systems of the dynamic
    positioning system and to provide alerts for critical system faults.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excursion limits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the excursion limits of
  a floating platform that is equipped with a dynamic positioning system
  are established based on the numerical analysis and model testing
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disconnect system

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a floating platform that is equipped with a
  dynamic positioning system has a disconnect system that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is capable of carrying out a planned disconnection
    of the floating platform from the seabed after allowing time to
    prepare the risers and subsea flowlines for the disconnection;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is capable of carrying out an emergency
    disconnection after allowing time to safely shut in wells and subsea
    equipment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} allows for reconnection in an orderly sequence, in
    the physical and environmental conditions described in the
    operations manual under paragraph 157(2)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demonstration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must periodically demonstrate by means
  of a trial or performance test that the disconnect system meets the
  requirements under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excursion limits exceeded

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the emergency
  disconnection referred to in paragraph (1)(b) is initiated if the
  floating platform exceeds the excursion limits established under
  subsection 149(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions and exemptions

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** For any floating
platform that is registered outside Canada, the operator must

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish a list of all flag State administration
  decisions and exemptions that apply to the floating platform in
  relation to any standards adopted by the International Maritime
  Organization;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct a risk assessment to identify measures to
  reduce the safety and environmental risks in relation to those
  decisions and exemptions to a level that is as low as reasonably
  practicable; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish an action plan to implement the measures
  referred to in paragraph (b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gap analysis

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** The operator must,
every time the MODU Code is updated,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} undertake a gap analysis between the criteria in the
  updated version and the version that applies to the floating platform
  based on its date of construction;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct a risk assessment in relation to any gaps
  identified; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that mitigation measures are implemented, as
  necessary.

#### [Asset Integrity]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1446601 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that all installations, including their systems and equipment, are
inspected, monitored, tested, maintained and operated to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure safety, the protection of the environment and
  the prevention of waste; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that they continue to perform in accordance
  with their design specifications under the operating conditions and
  maximum loads to which they may be subjected.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-destructive examination

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a non-destructive examination of the critical joints and structural
parts of an installation is conducted at least once every five years or
more often as required to ensure the continued safe operation of the
installation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrosion management

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that if a safety or environmental hazard would
  result from the failure due to corrosion --- including corrosion from
  exposure to a sour environment --- of any equipment, including process
  vessels, or of any piping, valves, fittings and structural elements
  that are part of an installation, that corrosion is prevented and
  managed throughout the life cycle of the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrosion management program

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must develop a corrosion management
  program that sets out the measures that are necessary to prevent
  critical failures resulting from corrosion-related degradation and to
  ensure the continued integrity of safety-critical elements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The program must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} identify all safety-critical elements that are
    susceptible to degradation by corrosion;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide for the analysis that is necessary to
    determine corrosion degradation mechanisms and the limits and
    failure modes of the safety-critical elements referred to in
    paragraph (a), taking into account the physical and environmental
    conditions and chemicals to which it is foreseeable that the
    safety-critical elements may be exposed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} include measures to prevent corrosion, as far as is
    practicable, and to mitigate or protect against the effects of
    corrosion;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide for the inspection and monitoring of
    corrosion and of any corrosion protection and prevention systems;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} provide for the collection and analysis of baseline
    and continuous data to monitor corrosion;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} provide for the continuous assessment, based on the
    data and analysis referred to in paragraph (e), of the maintenance
    activities and schedules referred to in paragraph 159(2)(f) to
    determine whether those activities and schedules are adequate to
    ensure corrosion management of safety-critical elements and provide
    for the modification of those activities and schedules, if
    necessary;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} provide for timely preventive maintenance of any
    corrosion protection and prevention systems; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} provide for the timely inspection, monitoring and
    maintenance of safety-critical elements in accordance with the
    requirements of the maintenance program referred to in paragraphs
    159(2)(e) and (f) and for any necessary repair before the limits
    established in paragraph (b) are reached.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program implementation and update

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the program is
  implemented and periodically updated, taking into account the data and
  analysis referred to in paragraph (3)(e).

#### [Operation and Maintenance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1446625 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits and requirements

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** An operator must
operate an installation, including its systems and equipment, in
accordance with any limitations that are set out in the certificate of
fitness under subsection 28(3), with any requirements under this Part
and with the operations manual referred to in section 157.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operations manual

- **[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must develop an operations manual in respect of each
  installation that sets out or incorporates by reference the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a general description of the installation,
    including its particular characteristics;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the chain of command and the roles,
    responsibilities and authorities of persons during normal operations
    of the installation and in emergency situations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a brief description of the systems and equipment on
    the installation, including flow sheets and instructions for the
    assembly, use and maintenance of the systems and equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the operating limits of the installation, including
    those of its systems and equipment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the physical and environmental conditions under
    which the installation and any pipeline can operate without
    compromising safety or the protection of the environment and the
    physical and environmental conditions that the installation and
    pipeline can withstand, taking into account the results of any
    analyses, tests, numerical modelling or investigations undertaken
    under subsection 105(2);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the results of the risk and reliability analysis
    conducted for the purpose of subsection 108(1);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a list of the procedures necessary to ensure the
    safe operation of the installation, including its systems and
    equipment, within the limits described in paragraph (d);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a list of the accidental events that would require
    implementing the contingency plan referred to in section 11, any
    possible triggers for such events and the measures to be implemented
    to avoid their occurrence;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a list of the procedures, practices, resources and
    monitoring measures set out in the contingency plan referred to in
    section 11;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the criteria for each platform's minimum
    penetration into the seabed or for the maximum scour of the
    platform's foundation and an indication of the arrangement of the
    platform's anchoring system;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a description of the characteristics of each
    platform's foundation and its penetration into the seabed or an
    indication of the arrangement of the platform's anchoring system, as
    well as a description of the measures to be implemented to monitor
    the integrity of the foundation or that of the mooring and anchoring
    systems;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the criteria to identify meteorological and
    oceanographic conditions and phenomena that require subsea
    components and pipelines to be inspected;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} plans that show the arrangement of watertight and
    weathertight subdivisions;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} details of openings in watertight and weathertight
    subdivisions, including the location of vents, air pipes and all
    other means of water penetration, and the means of closure of the
    compartments, as well as the location of downflooding points;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} a plan that contains information concerning
    permissible deck loads, variable loading limits and preloading;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} details of all audible and visual signals and
    alarms used in the communication system referred to in section 129,
    the general alarm system referred to in section 130, the fire and
    gas detection system referred to in section 132 and the emergency
    shutdown system referred to in section 133, as well as details of
    any colour-coding systems used for the safety of persons on the
    installation;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} information on any corrosion protection and
    prevention systems, including their type and location, and any
    requirements for the safety and maintenance of those systems;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} technical drawings that show

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the arrangement of any deck structure and of the
      equipment located on it, of all accommodations areas and temporary
      safe refuges and of any aircraft landing area, including its
      obstacle-free approach zone,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} sufficient details to permit verification and
      management, if applicable, of the integrity of hulls, mooring
      components, primary and critical structures, foundation elements,
      jacking mechanisms, risers and conductors,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the arrangement of hazardous areas and of any
      equipment located in those areas, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a fire control and evacuation plan, including

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the location of escape routes, fixed fire
        suppression systems and life-saving appliances, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the arrangement of barriers that provide
        passive fire and blast protection and associated equipment,
        along with a description of those barriers and equipment;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} the operating and maintenance requirements for all
    the life-saving appliances referred to in section 119;

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} information identifying the aircraft that were used
    for the design of any aircraft landing area on the installation and
    the maximum weight, size and wheel centres of those aircraft;

  - [(u)]{.lawlabel} any special arrangements in place to facilitate the
    inspection and maintenance of the installation, including its
    systems and equipment, and the storage of any crude oil on the
    installation;

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} special precautions to be taken or instructions to
    be followed when repairs or alterations to the installation,
    including its systems or equipment, are to be carried out;

  - [(w)]{.lawlabel} any special operational or emergency requirements
    and procedures with respect to any systems and equipment that are
    critical to safety, including the emergency shutdown system referred
    to in section 133;

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} a description of the air gap or freeboard and of
    the means of ensuring that the requirements under section 141 and
    subsections 142(2) and 145(1) and (3), as the case may be, are met;

  - [(y)]{.lawlabel} the number of persons who can be accommodated on
    the installation during normal operations;

  - [(z)]{.lawlabel} a description of the main electrical power supply
    referred to in subsection 122(4) and the emergency electrical power
    supply referred to in section 126 and any limitations on their
    operation;

  - [(z.1)]{.lawlabel} the procedure for periodically documenting the
    results of all inspections, monitoring, testing and maintenance of
    the installation's integrity, including the format and presentation
    of that documentation; and

  - [(z.2)]{.lawlabel} the procedure for notifying the Chief Safety
    Officer and the certifying authority under subsections 162(1) and
    170(1) and (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information --- floating
  platform

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a floating platform, the operations
  manual must also contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the platform's station-keeping
    system and its capabilities, taking into account the platform's
    operating limits;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all procedures for addressing the failure of any
    component of the station-keeping system that is critical to safety;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the station-keeping system is a mooring system,
    descriptions of the environmental loads that the moorings can
    sustain to keep the platform moored in place, the estimated holding
    power and capacity of the anchors in relation to the soil at the
    drill site or production site and the physical and environmental
    conditions in which reconnection of the platform is permitted;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the procedures for addressing an excursion outside
    of the limits established in the context of the analysis and model
    testing under subsections 147(2) and 149(2);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description and the limitations of any onboard
    computer or computer-based control systems used in operations such
    as ballasting and dynamic positioning and in the platform's trim and
    stability calculations;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} instructions on how to assess the loading and
    ballast conditions of the platform to determine its stability and
    how to manage those conditions to maintain the platform's stability
    in accordance with the provisions referred to in subsection 142(3);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} data on the location, type and weights of permanent
    ballast installed on the platform;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} hydrostatic curves or equivalent data;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a plan that shows the capacities and the centres of
    gravity of tanks and bulk material stowage compartments;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} tank-sounding tables or curves that show the
    capacities and the centres of gravity in graduated intervals and the
    free surface data for each tank;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} stability data that take into account the maximum
    height of the centre of gravity above the keel in relation to the
    draught curve and other parameters relevant to the stability of the
    platform;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the results of any inclining test, or of any
    lightweight survey together with the inclining test results, and the
    updated location of the platform centre of gravity following a
    deadweight survey;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} examples of loading conditions for each mode of
    operation, together with the means to evaluate any other loading
    conditions;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} technical drawings that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} show the arrangement and location of all openings
      that could affect the stability of the platform and their means of
      closure,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} show the arrangement and operation of the
      ballast and bilge systems,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} are accompanied by the operating instructions
      for the ballast and bilge systems, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} are sufficient in their scope and detail to
      ensure, in combination with the instructions referred to in
      subparagraph (iii), that

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the necessary draught, stability and hull
        strength can be maintained under all foreseeable operating
        conditions, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the floating platform can be returned to a safe
        condition from an unintended draught, trim or heel; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} a towing arrangement plan, if necessary, and the
    operating limits of the towing equipment's components.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information --- mobile
  offshore platform

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a self-elevating mobile offshore
  platform, the operations manual must also contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of any equipment for elevating and
    lowering the installation and details of any special types of joints
    and their purpose, including any operating or maintenance
    instructions for the equipment and joints; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the allowable limits for hull inclination and rack
    phase differential.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Up-to-date

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the operations manual
  is kept up-to-date.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Programs

- **[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must develop the following programs to ensure the continued
  integrity of an installation, including its systems and equipment,
  from the time the installation is commissioned until it is abandoned
  or removed from the offshore area:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance program referred to in section 159;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the preservation program referred to in section
    160; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the weight control program referred to in section
    161.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Program implementation and update

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the programs are
  implemented and periodically updated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maintenance program

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  maintenance program must set out the inspection, monitoring, testing
  and maintenance policies and procedures for the installation,
  including its systems and equipment, that are necessary to ensure
  safety, protect the environment and prevent waste.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The maintenance program must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} include the measures to ensure that the
    installation, including its systems and equipment, continues to
    perform in accordance with its design specifications;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include the measures to ensure compliance with any
    inspection, monitoring, testing or maintenance requirements under
    this Part;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} include the performance standards developed by the
    operator for the installation, including for its systems and
    equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} take into account the failure modes and mechanisms
    of safety-critical elements and the causes of their failure;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} include inspection and monitoring activities that
    occur at a frequency and in a manner to prevent, if practicable, the
    failures referred to in paragraph (d), or to mitigate the effects of
    those failures, and to ensure that safety-critical elements are
    repaired, replaced or modified without delay and in accordance with
    section 162; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} include predictive and preventive maintenance
    activities and schedules for each safety-critical element that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} are based on the performance standards referred
      to in paragraph (c),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} take into account the manufacturer's
      recommendations and industry standards and best practices,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} specify a minimum frequency for the
      comprehensive inspection of each safety-critical element, taking
      into account its condition and the conditions under which it is
      used,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} for rotating equipment, provide for partial or
      complete dismantling and inspection at a frequency necessary to
      maintain the equipment in good condition and to ensure that the
      equipment's functionality, availability, reliability and
      performance are in accordance with its design specifications,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} provide for a periodic maintenance regime for any
      low running-hour equipment, such as emergency generators,
      essential generators and fire pumps, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} provide for the management of spare parts so
      that critical spare parts are available on the installation to
      ensure the continued functionality, availability, reliability and
      performance of each safety-critical element in accordance with its
      design specifications.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preservation program

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  preservation program must set out the measures that are necessary to
  ensure the integrity of equipment that is taken out of service and
  stored for future use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periodic inspection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The program must provide for the periodic inspection
  of the stored equipment to verify its integrity and ensure that it is
  fit for the purposes for which it is to be used if it is brought into
  service.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Weight control program

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** The weight control
program must set out the measures that are necessary to ensure that the
weight and centre of gravity of each installation are kept safely within
the installation's operating limits.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety-critical element --- repair,
replacement or modification

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a certificate of fitness must ensure that the certifying
  authority and the Chief Safety Officer are notified before a
  safety-critical element is repaired, replaced or modified and before
  any equipment that would change the design, performance or integrity
  of a safety-critical element is brought on board the installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval before repair or modification
