  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The holder of a certificate of fitness must ensure
  that the approval of the certifying authority is obtained before a
  safety-critical element is repaired or modified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The holder of a certificate of fitness must ensure
  that a safety-critical element that has been repaired or modified is
  not put into operation until the certifying authority has verified it
  and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confirmed that it is fit for the purposes for which
    it is to be used, can be operated safely without posing a threat to
    persons or the environment and meets the requirements of these
    Regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} imposed any limitation on the operation of the
    installation that is necessary to ensure that the installation meets
    the requirements referred to in paragraph 28(1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency repair or modification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In an emergency, subsections (2) and (3) do not apply
  if the installation manager considers that the delay required to
  comply with the requirements under those subsections endangers persons
  on the installation or the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification after emergency

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A safety-critical element that is repaired or
  modified in an emergency must be verified by the certifying authority
  in accordance with subsection (3) as soon as the circumstances permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in the case of an
  adjustment made to or the testing of a boiler or pressure system
  fitting.

### [Wells]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1446741 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drilling fluid systems

**[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the drilling fluid system and associated monitoring
  equipment provide an effective barrier against formation pressure,
  ensure safe well operations, prevent pollution and allow for well
  evaluation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the indicators and alarms associated with the
  monitoring equipment are strategically located on the drilling rig to
  alert persons on it; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} dedicated personnel provide continuous monitoring,
  using independent monitoring systems, of parameters that are critical
  to safe well operations or to the detection of a gain or loss of
  drilling fluid while the installation is connected to the well and is
  taking fluid returns.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drilling riser

- **[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that every drilling riser is, throughout the
  duration of a well operation, capable of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} providing access to the well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} isolating the well-bore from the sea;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} withstanding the differential pressure of the
    drilling fluid relative to the sea;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} withstanding the maximum loads to which it may be
    subjected; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} permitting the drilling fluid to be returned to the
    installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drilling riser support

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that every drilling riser is
  supported in a manner that effectively compensates for any loads
  caused by the motion of the installation, the drilling fluid or the
  water column.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drilling riser analysis

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a drilling riser
  analysis and, in the case of a floating platform that uses a dynamic
  positioning system, a weak-point analysis of the drilling riser are
  conducted and that the certifying authority in relation to the
  installation approves those analyses.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fail-safe subsurface safety valves

- **[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a completed development well is equipped
  with a fail-safe subsurface safety valve that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} can be operated from the surface; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the well is located where permafrost is present
    in unconsolidated sediments, is installed in the production tubing
    below the base of the permafrost.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional valve

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a completed development
  well on a fixed platform that has gas-lift, injection or production
  capabilities in the A-annulus is equipped with an additional fail-safe
  safety valve on the A-annulus.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all fail-safe safety
  valves are designed, installed, tested, maintained and operated to
  prevent uncontrolled well flow when they are activated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Well tubulars, trees and wellheads

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that well tubulars, trees and wellheads are
  operated in accordance with good engineering practices.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sour environment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any well tubulars,
  trees or wellheads that may be exposed to a sour environment are
  capable of operating safely in that environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safe and efficient operation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the wellhead and tree
  equipment, including any valves, are designed and maintained to
  operate safely and efficiently throughout the life cycle of the well
  under all loads to which the well may be subjected.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation flow test equipment

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the equipment used in a formation flow test
  is designed to safely control well pressure, evaluate the formation
  and prevent pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rated working pressure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the rated working
  pressure of formation flow test equipment at and upstream of the well
  testing manifold exceeds the maximum anticipated shut-in pressure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overpressure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all equipment
  downstream of the well testing manifold is protected against
  overpressure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Downhole safety valve --- development
  well

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, in the case of a
  development well, that the formation flow test equipment includes a
  downhole safety valve that permits closure of the test string above
  the packer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Downhole safety valve --- exploratory
  or delineation well

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure, in the case of an
  exploratory well or a delineation well drilled on a geological
  feature, that a downhole safety valve is installed before a formation
  flow test is conducted unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it has been demonstrated as part of the formation
    flow test program referred to in paragraph 63(3)(a) that the level
    of risk of the proposed alternative arrangement in that program is
    equivalent to or lower than that of using a downhole safety valve;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Board has approved the test under subsection
    63(5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsea test tree

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any formation flow test
  equipment used in testing a well that is drilled with a floating
  drilling unit has a subsea test tree that is equipped with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a valve that can be operated from the surface and
    automatically closes when required to prevent uncontrolled well
    flow; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a release system that permits the test string to be
    hydraulically or mechanically disconnected within or below the
    blowout preventers.

### [Pipelines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1446792 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pipeline integrity --- standard

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a pipeline is designed, constructed,
  installed, operated and maintained in accordance with CSA Group
  standard Z662, Oil and gas pipeline systems, as it relates to offshore
  pipelines.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Integrity management program

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the pipeline system
  integrity management program required by that standard is implemented
  and periodically updated.

### [Monitoring of Installations, Wells and Pipelines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1446798 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring of systems

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that an installation is equipped with a central
  monitoring system in the main control centre to monitor all systems
  whose failure could cause or contribute to an accidental event or
  waste.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management of associated systems

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the alarm, safety,
  monitoring, warning and control functions associated with the systems
  that are monitored under subsection (1) are managed to prevent
  reportable incidents and waste.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of related system

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a function referred to in subsection (2) is
  suspended or found to be impaired, the operator must ensure that the
  use of any related system is also suspended until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a function that is suspended, that
    function is returned to service; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a function that was found to be
    impaired, measures are implemented to offset the risk of a
    reportable incident or waste.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Affected persons informed

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that all affected persons
  are informed when a function referred to in subsection (2) is
  suspended and when it is returned to service.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deterioration

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must, without delay, notify the Chief Safety Officer of any
  deterioration of an installation, including its systems or equipment,
  or of a pipeline, well, vessel or support craft if that deterioration
  could adversely affect safety or the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to certifying authority

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an installation, system, equipment, pipeline or
  part of a well referred to in subsection (1) is within the scope of
  work referred to in section 31, the operator must also, without delay,
  notify the certifying authority of the deterioration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Impairment rectification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any impairment of an
  installation, including its systems or equipment, or of a pipeline,
  well, vessel or support craft is rectified without delay if the
  impairment could adversely affect safety or the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mitigation measures

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If it is not possible to rectify the impairment
  without delay, the operator must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct a risk assessment to identify risk
    mitigation measures;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} implement those mitigation measures; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the impairment is rectified as soon as
    the circumstances permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (3) and (4) do not apply in respect of
  safety-critical elements.

## [PART 11]{.HLabel1}[Support Operations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1446824 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Support craft

- **[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must, in respect of an installation on which persons are
  normally present, ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a support craft that is at a distance from the
    installation not greater than that required for a return time of 20
    minutes is available at all times for emergency response; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whenever an aircraft is landing or taking off, or
    personnel are working over the side or otherwise exposed to the risk
    of falling in the water, a support craft is available in the
    immediate vicinity of the installation and ready to undertake rescue
    and recovery operations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The support craft referred to in subsection (1) must
  be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} capable of safely providing necessary support
    functions in the foreseeable physical and environmental conditions
    prevailing in the area in which it operates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} equipped to supply emergency services, including
    rescue and first aid treatment, for all personnel on the
    installation in the event of an emergency; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} equipped with a self-righting fast rescue boat that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} meets the requirements under chapter V of the LSA
      Code,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is capable of being launched and retrieved when
      it is loaded with a full complement of persons and equipment, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is ready for deployment in the event of an
      emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required distance exceeded

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the support craft is located at a distance that
  exceeds the distance referred to in paragraph (1)(a), both the
  installation manager and the person in charge of the support craft
  must log that fact and the reason why the distance or time was
  exceeded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vessel master

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} During any activity or situation referred to in
  paragraph (1)(b), or any other activity or situation that presents an
  increased level of risk to the safety of the installation, the vessel
  master must, under the direction of the installation manager, keep the
  craft in close proximity to the installation, maintain open
  communication channels with the installation and be prepared to
  conduct rescue operations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rescue boat --- vessel

**[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** An operator must, in
respect of any vessel that is used in a geoscientific program,
geotechnical program, environmental program, diving project or
construction activity, ensure that a rescue boat is available and ready
for use in the event of an emergency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety zone

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  support craft must not enter the safety zone around an installation or
  around a vessel that is engaged in a geoscientific program,
  geotechnical program, environmental program or diving project without
  the consent of the installation manager or the person in charge of the
  operations site.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to approaching aircraft or
  vessel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that persons who are in
  charge of an aircraft or vessel that is approaching the safety zone
  are notified of the safety zone boundaries and of any hazards within
  that zone that relate to the operator's installation or vessel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Boundaries --- installation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The safety zone around an installation consists of
  the area within a line that encloses the installation and is drawn at
  a distance of 500 m from the outer edge of the installation or, if any
  component of the installation extends beyond that edge, from the outer
  limit of the component that extends furthest from that edge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Boundaries --- vessel

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The safety zone around a vessel referred to in
  subsection (1) consists of the area within a line that encloses the
  vessel and any of its attached equipment and is drawn at a distance
  that minimizes risks to safety, the environment and property located
  nearby, including fishing gear or fishing vessels.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Landing area

- **[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that the aircraft landing area on an installation
  or vessel and the equipment that is used in that area or that
  otherwise supports the take-off or landing of aircraft are designed to
  ensure safety and the protection of the environment and to prevent
  incidents or damage resulting from the use of aircraft.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that the landing area

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has an obstacle-free take-off and approach area and
    is oriented relative to prevailing winds;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} can withstand all functional loads imposed by
    aircraft;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} can accommodate aircraft of expected sizes;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} has emergency response and firefighting equipment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} has conspicuous markings and signage;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} has adequate lighting, including in reduced
    visibility conditions;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} has monitoring and status light systems and
    communication and meteorological equipment;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} is readily and safely accessible, including from
    the accommodations areas and from any temporary safe refuge; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a landing area on an installation,
    is equipped with fuel storage tanks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fuel storage tanks

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that any fuel storage tanks
  that are in proximity to a landing area are stored safely and
  protected from damage, impact and fire.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedures

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** The operator must
ensure the establishment of procedures associated with the support of
aircraft operations, including procedures for emergency response, and of
a training program for personnel for those purposes.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aircraft service provider

**[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that, before the start of any operations that require the use of an
aircraft, the aircraft service provider has accepted in writing all
conditions with respect to the use of the equipment in any landing area,
the procedures associated with the support of aircraft operations,
including the procedures for emergency response, and the training
program for personnel in respect of those matters.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classification

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that any support or construction vessel to be used in conjunction with
an installation holds a valid certificate of class issued by a
classification society according to the work or activity to be carried
out by it.

## [PART 12]{.HLabel1}[Notice, Records, Reports and Other Information for Authorized Works and Activities]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1446875 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1446876 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[shotpoint]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[shotpoint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the surface location of a
seismic energy source.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reportable incidents

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must notify the Board of a reportable incident as soon as the
  circumstances permit, but not later than 24 hours after becoming aware
  of the incident.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any reportable incident is investigated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person who conducts the investigation includes
    in their investigation report the root causes of the reportable
    incident, the contributing factors, measures to be implemented to
    prevent its recurrence and any other relevant information; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the investigation report is submitted to the Board
    not later than 14 days after the day on which the reportable
    incident occurred.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessibility of records

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that any records that are necessary to support operational requirements
and the requirements of these Regulations are readily accessible to the
Board for examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Critical information

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that records are kept of all information ---
  including the following --- that is critical to safety, the protection
  of the environment or the prevention of waste:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} information on the location and movement of support
    craft;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information on reportable incidents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} information on emergency drills and exercises;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} information on the quantities of consumable
    substances at any operations site;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} data resulting from any required observation of
    wildlife;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} information on verification, inspection,
    monitoring, testing, maintenance and operating activities;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} information on the status of all systems and
    equipment identified in the safety plan as being critical to safety
    or in the environmental protection plan as being critical to the
    protection of the environment, including any test result indicating
    that the systems or equipment are not functioning as intended and
    information on any equipment failure that has led to an impairment
    of any of those systems; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} information on the physical and environmental
    conditions observed and forecasted under section 42.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention periods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must retain the records referred to in
  subsection (1) for the following periods:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in
    paragraphs (1)(a) and (e) to (g) and those referred to in paragraph
    (1)(h) regarding forecasted conditions, five years after the day on
    which the record is created;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in
    paragraphs (1)(b) and (c),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} five years after the day on which the drill or
      exercise is carried out, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 10 years after the day on which the reportable
      incident is reported;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in paragraph
    (1)(d), for as long as the consumable substance is at the operations
    site; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in paragraph
    (1)(h) regarding conditions observed, for the duration of the
    authorized work or activity.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Safety report

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a safety report that relates to an
  authorized work or activity conducted in a given calendar year is
  submitted to the Board within 90 days after the day on which the work
  or activity is concluded or suspended or, in the case of a work or
  activity that will continue into the following calendar year, that a
  safety report that relates to the work or activity conducted in the
  preceding calendar year is submitted to the Board not later than March
  31 of that following calendar year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The safety report must contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description and analysis of the efforts
    undertaken to improve safety;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the operator's safety performance
    during the applicable calendar year, including with respect to the
    objective of reducing safety risks;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the safety measures and actions taken
    to mitigate the effects of any reportable incident, as well as of
    their effectiveness and any adjustments made for their continued
    improvement; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any emergency response drills and
    exercises relating to safety that were completed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual reports

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that the Board is made aware, at least once a year, of any report
containing relevant information regarding applied research work or
studies that the operator has participated in, funded or commissioned
concerning the operator's authorized works and activities in relation to
safety, the protection of the environment or resource management and
must ensure that a copy of the report is submitted to the Board on
request.

### [Geoscientific, Geotechnical and Environmental Programs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1446919 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- key dates

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** When any geoscientific
program, geotechnical program or environmental program is commenced,
concluded, suspended or cancelled by an operator, the operator must,
without delay, notify the Board in writing of the date of the
commencement, conclusion, suspension or cancellation of the program.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Weekly status reports

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that weekly reports are submitted to the Board on
  the status of field work carried out in relation to any geoscientific
  program, geotechnical program or environmental program from the
  commencement of the program until its conclusion, suspension or
  cancellation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of reports

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The weekly status reports must contain the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the program number assigned by the Board;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information identifying, and indicating the current
    location and status of, all operations sites and support craft used
    in the program;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} key dates of the works and activities under the
    program, in particular their commencement, suspension and completion
    dates,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the works and activities carried
    out during the preceding week, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of data collected, broken down by
      data acquisition technique,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} information identifying and indicating the
      location of data collection points, lines or areas,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a schedule indicating each type of work or
      activity carried out under the program, as well as any period in
      which data acquisition was delayed or interrupted, along with a
      summary of the causes of that delay or interruption, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a description of any failure to comply with a
      condition of the authorization;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} maps illustrating, in relation to the proposed data
    acquisition plan referred to in subparagraph 8(i)(iii), the portion
    of the data acquisition that has been completed, the portion that
    was completed in the preceding week and the portion that remains to
    be carried out;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the total number of persons
    involved in the program who, during the preceding week, were at, or
    transferred to or from, the operations sites and, if applicable, the
    means by which they were transferred;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any communications or interactions
    that occurred during the preceding week concerning program
    activities between persons associated with the program and persons
    associated with fishing activities;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a summary of emergency drills and exercises that
    were completed and reportable incidents that occurred during the
    preceding week;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the quantities of consumable
    substances that are critical to safety that are currently at each
    operations site;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} all wildlife observation data from the preceding
    week that were required to be recorded under paragraph 181(1)(e);

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the verification, inspection,
    monitoring, testing, maintenance and operating activities that are
    critical to safety that were carried out during the preceding week;
    and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} a description of any measures taken during the
    preceding week to avoid disturbing wildlife or interfering with
    fishing activities or any other uses of the sea.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental report --- programs

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that an environmental report that contains the following documents and
information is submitted to the Board within 90 days after the day on
which a geoscientific program, geotechnical program or environmental
program is concluded or suspended:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the general physical and
  environmental conditions under which the program was conducted and, if
  applicable, a description of ice management activities and
  non-productive time caused by meteorological or ice conditions;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a summary of program performance in relation to the
  environment, including with respect to the objective of reducing
  environmental risks;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a summary of environmental protection measures and
  actions that were taken to mitigate the effects of any reportable
  incident, as well as of their effectiveness and any adjustments made
  for their continued improvement;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any emergency response drills and
  exercises for the protection of the environment that were completed;
  and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} all wildlife observation data that were required to
  be recorded under paragraph 181(1)(e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final reports

- **[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a final operations report, final data
  processing report and final interpretation report are submitted to the
  Board with the acquired data referred to in subsection (5), as
  applicable, within 12 months after the day on which any geoscientific
  program, geotechnical program or environmental program is concluded,
  unless a longer period has been agreed to in writing by the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of final operations report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The final operations report must contain the
  following documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the program number assigned by the Board;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the title, author and date of the report;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an executive summary and table of contents;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the names of the operator, contractors and any
    [[interest owner]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 49
    of the Act;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of all operation sites and any
    support craft used for the program;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a description of the program, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} key dates, in particular its commencement,
      suspension and completion dates,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the equipment used,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the operational methods employed,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the number of persons who were involved in the
      program, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of data collected, broken down by
      data acquisition technique;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} location maps illustrating details of the data
    acquisition activities carried out under the program, including the
    identification and location of data collection points, lines or
    areas and the type of data acquired;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} location maps illustrating the boundaries of each
    area covered by the program and any portion of those areas that is
    subject to an [[interest]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
    section 49 of the Act, as well as the identification number of each
    such interest;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a schedule that specifies the type and duration of
    all program activities and includes any period in which data
    acquisition was delayed or interrupted;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an indication as to the accuracy of the navigation
    system and of the positioning and survey systems, as well as the
    parameters and configuration of both the energy source and recording
    system; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} shotpoint maps, track plots, flight lines with
    numbered fiducial points, gravity station maps, location maps for
    any samples or core holes, copies of any photographs and a list of
    any videos.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of final data processing report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The final data processing report must contain the
  following documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the documents and information referred to in
    paragraphs (2)(a) to (d), (g) and (k);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the program, including the
    quantity of data collected, broken down by data acquisition
    technique; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the geoscientific data acquired,
    including the data processing sequence and parameters.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of final interpretation report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The final interpretation report must contain the
  following documents and information, as applicable:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the documents and information referred to in
    paragraphs (2)(a) to (e);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} bathymetric or topographic maps compiled from the
    data collected;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description and interpretive maps of the acquired
    data, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} time and depth structure and isopach maps,
      velocity and residual velocity maps and seismic attribute maps,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} final Bouguer gravity maps and any residual or
      other processed gravity maps,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} final total magnetic intensity contour maps and
      any residual, gradient or other processed magnetic maps,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} final controlled-source electromagnetic
      resistivity maps,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} surficial maps generated from any seabed,
      geohazard or pipeline route survey, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} any geological maps;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description and analysis of the interpretation of
    the data with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} geological and geophysical correlations,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} correlations between gravity, magnetic, seismic
      and controlled-source electromagnetic data, including correlations
      to any data acquired during previous surveys,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of seabed surveys, the geophysical
      correlation between shallow seismic data and data from cores and
      geotechnical boreholes,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} corrections or adjustments that were applied to
      the data during processing or compilation,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the velocity information that the operator used
      in a time-to-depth conversion,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} cores and samples,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} other geoscientific and geotechnical analyses,
      and

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} geohazards; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} synthetic seismograms,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} seismic modelling studies that use synthetic
      seismograms,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} vertical seismic profiles at wells that were
      used in the interpretation of the data,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} amplitude versus offset studies,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} seismic inversion studies, if any, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} any other seismic studies related to the
      program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquired data

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The following acquired data must accompany the final
  reports, as applicable:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} time-stamped track plot, shotpoint and sample
    location data;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} bathymetric data;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all final processed seismic data for each 2-D
    seismic line in time and depth;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all final processed 3-D volumes and each line
    generated from that volume in time and depth;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any vertical seismic profiles, synthetic
    seismograms, amplitude versus offset data and seismic inversion
    data;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any seabed, geohazard or pipeline
    route survey,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} processed high-resolution data for each line,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} digital location maps for any samples,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any photographs and videos, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} sub-bottom profiler and side-scan sonar data;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an environmental program, any
    photograph, video or other graphic information that is relevant and
    contributes to the interpretation of the final data and the drafting
    of the final report;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a gravity or magnetic survey, a
    series of gravity and magnetic profiles across all gravity and
    magnetic surveys; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of controlled-source electromagnetic
    data, final processed cross-sections on all receiver lines, curves
    from all receivers and all 2-D and 3-D final models generated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation of previous data

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The operator must incorporate into any map referred
  to in paragraph (4)(b) that is included in a final interpretation
  report any data previously collected by the operator that are related
  to the area covered by the map and that are of a type similar to the
  data from which the map was produced.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- data made available to
public

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator that has conducted a geoscientific program, a geotechnical
  program or an environmental program need not submit a final
  interpretation report if the data acquired from the program are made
  available to the public for purchase or for use under licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data no longer available

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the operator ceases to make data available for
  purchase or use under licence, the operator must ensure that, within
  12 months after the day on which the operator ceased to make the data
  available, the final interpretation report is submitted to the Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Data purchases

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  purchaser of data referred to in subsection 188(1) that were acquired
  in an area that is subject to an [[interest]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
  as defined in section 49 of the Act, must submit to the Board a final
  interpretation report that contains the documents and information
  referred to in subsection 187(4), as applicable, if the costs of the
  purchase of the data are to be credited against a deposit or other
  costs in relation to the interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports from data purchaser

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the purchaser has reprocessed or reinterpreted the
  data, and if the costs of the reprocessing or reinterpretation are to
  be credited against a deposit or other costs of the interest, the
  purchaser must submit to the Board, with the acquired data referred to
  in subsection 187(5), as applicable, a final data processing report
  that contains the documents and information referred to in subsection
  187(3) and a final interpretation report that contains the documents
  and information referred to in subsection 187(4), as applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Timing of submissions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The reports and data required under subsections (1)
  and (2) must be submitted by the purchaser to the Board before the
  costs referred to in those subsections are credited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Chief Conservation Officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who has submitted a report referred to in
  this section must, in respect of data that pertain to shotpoints or
  the location of stations, notify the Chief Conservation Officer,
  without delay, of any errors or omissions identified in or corrections
  made to the data after the report is submitted.

### [Drilling and Production]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1447034 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** When submitting any
information to the Board about a well, pool, zone or field under these
Regulations, an operator must refer to each by the name given to it
under section 59 or paragraph 60(b), as the case may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Results, data, analyses and schematics

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a copy of the final results, data, analyses
  and schematics obtained from any well operation, including those
  obtained as a result of the following activities, is submitted to the
  Board:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the testing, sampling and pressure measurements
    that are conducted as part of the field data acquisition program
    referred to in section 13 and the well data acquisition program
    referred to in section 18, as well as any evaluation, testing and
    sampling of formations that is conducted under section 62; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any verification conducted under paragraph 71(2)(a)
    and any segregation test conducted under paragraph 71(2)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for submission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise agreed to in writing by the Board,
  the operator must ensure that the copy is submitted within 60 days
  after the day on which the activity that gave rise to the results,
  data, analyses or schematics is concluded.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Survey

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a survey, certified by a person licensed
  under the [Canada Lands Surveyors Act](/eng/acts/L-5.8), is conducted
  to confirm the location of any well and production installation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of survey plan

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must ensure that a copy of the survey
  plan is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} filed with the Canada Lands Survey Records; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} submitted to the Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Critical information

- **[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  records that must be kept under section 181 include, in the case of an
  operation involving drilling or production, records containing the
  following information and documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any assessment of the efficacy of a
    spill-treating agent under paragraph 11(4)(a),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a description of the assessment, including any
      oil samples used, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a description of any tests conducted for the
      assessment and their results;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information concerning the inspection of any
    installation and its equipment or a pipeline for corrosion and
    erosion and any resulting maintenance activities carried out;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the pressure, temperature and flow rate data
    obtained from compressors and from systems and equipment used for
    treatment and processing;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} information concerning the calibration of meters
    and other instruments on an installation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} information concerning the testing of subsea,
    surface and subsurface safety valves;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} information concerning the status of each well and
    the status of well operations;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a floating platform, information
    concerning all loads that could affect the motions, stability or
    inclination of the platform, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} data, observations, measurements and calculations
      related to its stability and station-keeping capability, including
      records of all of its movements,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the results of all tests and analyses conducted
      to assess its stability and station-keeping capability,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a description of every change in relation to
      its weight, its centre of gravity or the weight or distribution of
      temporary or portable equipment on it that may affect its
      stability, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a description of the verification of the
      disconnect capability of any disconnectable mooring system;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in respect of boilers and pressure systems, the
    documents and information referred to in paragraphs 135(12)(d) to
    (f);

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} information concerning each formation leak-off test
    and formation integrity test conducted under section 70;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the findings resulting from the verifications of
    temporary safe refuges required under subsection 117(3); and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the findings resulting from the verifications of
    the availability and condition of life-saving appliances required
    under subsection 119(11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention periods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The operator must retain the records referred to in
  subsection (1) for the following periods:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in paragraph
    (1)(a), for as long as the spill-treating agent is approved for use;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in
    paragraphs (1)(b) to (f), subparagraph (1)(g)(iv) and paragraphs
    (1)(i) to (k), five years after the day on which the record is
    created;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in
    subparagraphs (1)(g)(i) to (iii), for the life of the floating
    platform; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the records referred to in paragraph
    (1)(h), five years after the day on which the boiler or pressure
    system is taken out of service.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Daily production record

- **[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that a daily production record is kept in respect
  of a field in which a pool or well is located until the field is
  abandoned and, at that time, must offer to submit the record to the
  Board before destroying it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The daily production record must contain, with
  respect to each day, the following information and documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} information concerning the calibration of meters
    and other instruments referred to in paragraph 193(1)(d);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any measurements obtained under section 74;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the manner in which any fluids
    were disposed of, including through venting, burning or flaring, or
    transported for processing, whether through offloading or a
    pipeline; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other information relating to the production of
    petroleum and other fluids from each pool or well.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation flow test records and report

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of exploratory wells and delineation
  wells, a record of formation flow test results is submitted to the
  Board on a daily basis; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of all wells, a formation flow test report
  is submitted to the Board as soon as the circumstances permit after
  each formation flow test.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pilot scheme

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that interim evaluations of a pilot scheme
  referred to in section 81 are reported to the Board in writing at the
  intervals referred to in paragraph 81(2)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report on completion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the pilot scheme, the operator must
  ensure that a report is submitted to the Board that contains

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the results of the scheme and supporting data and
    analyses; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operator's conclusions as to the potential of
    the scheme for application to full-scale production.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Daily reports

**[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that the following reports are submitted to the Board on a daily basis:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a daily operations report that contains

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a description of the works and activities that were
    carried out on the installation on the previous day and the current
    status of those works and activities,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a description of the works and activities that are
    expected to be carried out on the installation on the day on which
    the report is submitted,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the verification, inspection,
    monitoring, testing, maintenance and operating activities critical
    to safety that were carried out on the previous day,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the physical and environmental
    conditions that were observed under section 42 on the previous day,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the information referred to in
    paragraph 193(1)(g) that was obtained on the previous day, and

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} any other information that is necessary to
    indicate the status of operations on the installation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a daily drilling report that contains

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the daily and cumulative costs of operating the
    installation,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} all well and casing data obtained on the previous
    day,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a description of the properties of the drilling
    fluid and all drilling fluid gas readings from the previous day,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any directional and deviation surveys
    carried out the previous day,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a description of the formations encountered on the
    previous day,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the results of any blowout preventer test carried
    out on the previous day and the date of the most recent test, and

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the results of any formation leak-off tests or
    formation integrity tests referred to in section 70 that were
    carried out the previous day;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a daily geological report, consisting of well and
  field data acquired the previous day through the programs referred to
  in sections 13 and 18 , geological assessments made the previous day
  and any other information that is relevant to those assessments; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a production installation, a daily
  production report that contains a summary of the information referred
  to in paragraphs 193(1)(a) to (c) in relation to the previous day and
  a summary of the daily production record referred to in section 194.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monthly production report

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a report summarizing the production data collected during a given
month is submitted to the Board not later than the 15th day of the
subsequent month.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Well records and reports

- **[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a well termination record is submitted to the Board
    in respect of a well within 21 days after

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the well is abandoned,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the well is suspended if the
      suspension is planned to be for a period that is longer than 21
      days, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the well is completed or
      recompleted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a well operation report is submitted to the Board
    in respect of a well that requires a workover or intervention within
    30 days after the day on which the workover or intervention is
    completed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a well history report is submitted to the Board in
    respect of a development well within 45 days after the day referred
    to in subparagraph (a)(i), (ii) or (iii), as the case may be;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a well history report is submitted to the Board in
    respect of an exploration or delineation well within 90 days after
    the day referred to in subparagraph (a)(i), (ii) or (iii), as the
    case may be; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the actual cost breakdown of all well operations is
    submitted to the Board within 90 days after the day on which a well
    is abandoned, suspended or completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Well termination record --- contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The record required under paragraph (1)(a) must
  describe the manner in which the well has been abandoned, suspended,
  completed or recompleted and must include a schematic of the well
  illustrating the nature and location of the plugs used to abandon or
  suspend the well or the equipment used to complete or recomplete the
  well.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports --- contents

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The reports required under paragraphs (1)(b) to (d)
  must contain a record of all operational, engineering, petrophysical,
  geophysical and geological information that is relevant to the well
  operation, including any problems encountered during the well
  operation and the results of any formation leak-off test or formation
  integrity test conducted under section 70.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Impact description

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The report required under paragraph (1)(b) must
  describe any impact of the workover or intervention on the performance
  of the well, including any effect on productivity, injectivity and the
  recovery of petroleum.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental report --- drilling

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** An operator must
ensure, in relation to a drilling program that involves an exploratory
well or a delineation well, that an environmental report that contains
the following documents and information is submitted to the Board within
90 days after the day referred to in subparagraph 199(1)(a)(i), (ii) or
(iii), as the case may be:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the physical and environmental
  conditions under which the drilling program was conducted and, if
  applicable, a description of ice management activities and
  non-productive time caused by meteorological or ice conditions;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the environmental protection measures in
  place during the drilling program, the measures taken to mitigate the
  effects of any reportable incident, the effectiveness of those
  measures and any adjustments made for their continued improvement;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the performance of the drilling program
  in relation to the environment, including in relation to the objective
  of reducing environmental risks;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any emergency response drills and
  exercises for the protection of the environment that were completed;
  and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} all wildlife observation data that was required to be
  recorded under paragraph 181(1)(e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual environmental report ---
production and pipeline

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** An operator must
ensure, in relation to a production project or pipeline project, that an
environmental report that contains the following documents and
information with respect to a given calendar year is submitted to the
Board not later than March 31 of the subsequent year:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the general physical and environmental
  conditions to which each operations site was subjected;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of any ice management activities
  carried out;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the environmental protection measures in
  place, the measures taken to mitigate the effects of any reportable
  incident, the effectiveness of those measures and any adjustments made
  for their continued improvement;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the performance of the project in
  relation to the environment, including in relation to the objective of
  reducing environmental risks;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any emergency response drills and
  exercises for the protection of the environment that were completed;
  and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} all wildlife observation data that were required to
  be recorded under paragraph 181(1)(e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual production report

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that, not later than March 31 of each year, an annual production report
for a pool, field or zone is submitted to the Board that contains
information on how the operator manages and intends to manage the
resource being produced without waste, including

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the preceding calendar year, details on
  performance, production forecasts, reserve revision, the reasons for
  deviations in well performance from forecasts in previous annual
  production reports, gas conservation resources, efforts to maximize
  the recovery of petroleum and operating and capital expenditures,
  including the cost of each well operation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the preceding calendar year, the current calendar
  year and the next two calendar years, capital costs and fixed
  operating costs for each well and field in a production project,
  variable costs, commodity prices and financial commitments in relation
  to the transportation of the resource, including by pipeline.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gas venting records

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a record is kept of the following information in respect of each
gas venting referred to in paragraph 82(c):

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the emergency situation that
  justified the venting;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the venting, the date it occurred
  and its duration; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the volume of gas vented.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compressor records

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a record containing the following documents and information is kept
in respect of the compressors referred to in subsection 84(1):

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} information demonstrating, with supporting documents,
  that the continuous monitoring device referred to in subsection 84(2)
  has been calibrated in accordance with the manufacturer's
  recommendations such that its measurements have a maximum margin of
  error of ±10%; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for each compressor, if its maximum flow rate limit
  under subsection 84(3) or (4) has been exceeded,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} its serial number, make and model,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the maximum flow rate limit was
    exceeded,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the flow rate indicated by the continuous
    monitoring device when the maximum flow rate limit was exceeded, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a description of the corrective measures that were
    taken and the dates on which they was taken.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fugitive emission records

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a record containing the following information is kept in respect of
any fugitive emission from an installation that is detected:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the emission was detected;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of equipment from which the emission was
  released and its location within the installation or identifier;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the means by which the emission was identified; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the corrective measures that were
  taken and the dates on which they were taken.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record retention period

**[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** An operator must ensure
that a record referred to in any of sections 203 to 205 is retained for
five years after the day on which the record is created.

### [Diving Projects or Construction Activities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1447175 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Weekly status reports

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operator must ensure that weekly reports are submitted to the Board on
  the status of any diving project or construction activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of reports

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The weekly status reports must contain the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the project number assigned by the Board;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information identifying, and indicating the current
    location and status of, all operations sites and support craft used
    in the diving project or construction activities;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the works and activities carried
    out during the preceding week;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the total number of persons
    involved in the works and activities who, during the week, were at,
    or transferred to or from, the operations sites and, if applicable,
    the means by which they were transferred;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a summary of emergency drills and exercises that
    were completed and reportable incidents that occurred during the
    week;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the quantities of consumable
    substances that are critical to safety that are currently at each
    operations site; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the verification, inspection,
    monitoring, testing, maintenance and operating activities that are
    critical to safety that were carried out during the preceding week.

## [PART 13]{.HLabel1}[Repeals and Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1447188 .Part}

### [Coming into Force]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1447195 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eight months after publication

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_SOR-2024-26_e_transform_hq_17090640033){#fn_SOR-2024-26_e_transform_hq_17090640033-ID0EBCA}[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209
.sectionLabel}** These Regulations come into force on the day that, in
the eighth month after the month in which they are published in the
[Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), Part II, has the same
calendar number as the day on which they are published or, if that
eighth month has no day with that number, the last day of that eighth
month.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_SOR-2024-26_e_transform_hq_17090640033-ID0EBCA){#fn_SOR-2024-26_e_transform_hq_17090640033}\[Note:
  Regulations in force October 28, 2024.\]
  :::
